Val wants the **home-screen icons restyled** (thinner or no border, larger icons, closer to Light & Wonder / Aristocrat) so players stop recognizing a JVL game "at a distance" — applied to Calle Ocho and JPRE and any new game. Juan to keep an optional one-/three-/five-line game submitted-and-approved "in his back pocket" (Val indifferent). The **POS January rollout** gives Juan a product (not sales) pitch when he sits with operators from November. [[raw/review_games_concepts_markets-september-18,2025.md]]

## Nov 20 2025 — depart from Peach: vertical liquid-glass multi-game (6+5+6, 12 enabled, 3 progressives)

On the [[wiki/meetings/2025-11-20-games-development-review-georgia-liquid-glass-multi-game.md|Nov-20 games review]] (Val, [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]], with [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]]), Felipe argued the 5 **Jackpot Rush** titles all feel the same (25-line, center-reel-nudge) and Georgia players want variety in reel format / line count / features (Aristocrat & Light & Wonder mix 1-line up to 1024-ways; the popular Mo Money is a **one-line** game there). Resolved: build a **vertical liquid-glass multi-game** combining **6 Peach/lines-and-ways + 5 Jackpot Rush + 6 Fiery Ice Link** titles as **three categories, each with its own independent progressive**; **submit all, enable 12 on screen**, operators toggling via **RIS** to cut Eclipse submission cost. Juan to write the REQ and adjust Jira; Val corrected it to **concept stage**. [[raw/Games_development_review (1).md]]

- **Retire the "Peach" brand and drop the Georgia Lottery logo** — use the liquid-glass launch as the clean break; new theme to be neutral / "aspirational wealth," explicitly not Georgia-native/historical (players largely Hispanic / African-American / South American), and repurposable in Colombia. Theme TBD. [[raw/Games_development_review (1).md]]
- **Val's pricing angle:** enable all games and **raise the price ~$2,000**, framed on the tariff increases buyers expect this year; start with vertical, then revisit dual-screen. [[raw/Games_development_review (1).md]]
- **Market math driving it:** ~**35,000 machines / ~6,500 locations** (~5.5/location) with 5–6 brands now (IGT, L&W, Aristocrat, JVL, Novomatic, newly Konami); JVL now targets **one machine per location**, so a larger per-machine library matters — tempered by the "arms race" and "paradox of choice" risk (12 is "aplenty"). [[raw/Games_development_review (1).md]]
- **Nebraska essentially licensed** (approval draft letter received; one affidavit left; ~Dec-1 selling target), and **South Dakota** is JVL's #1 win-per-unit market on only 6 games, with a proposed 7-game South Dakota slate. See the meeting stub. [[raw/Games_development_review.md]]

## Feb 9 2026 — Diamond Amusement partnership + performance/data-reliability review

A two-part Feb-9-2026 arc with the Georgia master operator **[[wiki/organizations/diamond-amusement.md|Diamond Amusement]]** (CEO [[wiki/people/sean-fellows.md|Sean Fellows]]): a strategic **partnership meeting** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-09-diamond-amusement-partnership-meeting.md]]) and a JVL-internal **exec debrief** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-09-diamond-amusement-exec-debrief.md]]) the same evening.

- **Competitive standing (Diamond's data).** JVL's 41 units at Diamond average **$167 win-per-unit/day** vs a **Georgia state average of ~$108** (down from a COVID-era ~$137; state now runs **41,000+ machines**). Benchmark: IGT "Price is Right" **$221**, full IGT **$176**, **Aristocrat $305** (top-tier only), **Novomatic $169**. The "big three" (Aristocrat/IGT/Light & Wonder) dominate; JVL sits in the next tier; **Konami exited** Georgia. New entrants use **94–96% RTP** (JVL H3 = 92%) as a share-grab tactic. Full detail on [[wiki/organizations/diamond-amusement.md]]. [[raw/Diamond_Shawn_Fellows_interview-February-9-2026.md]]
- **New-nudge underperformance finding.** JVL's newer "enhanced nudge" software is performing **~50% below** the older software at Diamond (roughly $80 vs the $167 average). Only ~10% of Diamond's JVL cabinets carry it, mostly units that came back to the warehouse for **TITO/printer retrofit** before redeployment. Michael argued the change was only graphical (nudge communication), so a 50% drop "doesn't seem logical." Cause unresolved — recorded as an open question below. [[raw/Exec_recap_Diamond_Amusement-February-9-2026.md]]
- **Intralot/GLC reports judged unreliable.** The debrief found impossible meters (billions/trillions wagered, negative game profit) traced to an old **Mother Goose** pay-table-ID misconfiguration that is never cleaned up (Intralot has no audit department; the longer a manufacturer has been in-market, the dirtier its data). The **Co-am ID intentionally obscures machine serial and physical location**, so JVL cannot tie a report line to a place. Example machines: ID 258 ~**$899,643 coin-in over 187 days** (~$209/day reported, ~$385 at 8% hold) vs ID 206 at **~$16.48/day** — different, unknowable location tiers. [[raw/Exec_recap_Diamond_Amusement-February-9-2026.md]]
- **Data-sharing partnership (the fix).** JVL proposed placing machines in **tier-one locations alongside IGT/L&W/Aristocrat** and across all three tiers, trading **free trial machines, extended warranty, spare-parts kits, and first access to new games** (Fiery Ice Link, Jackpot Rush) for granular operational data. Sean agreed, offered historical baselines ($80/$140/$500 example locations), and to facilitate field visits. Val's directive to Felipe: stop relying on operator hearsay — get **into the locations** and take the numbers himself, biweekly. [[raw/Diamond_Shawn_Fellows_interview-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/Exec_recap_Diamond_Amusement-February-9-2026.md]]
- **Transactional suite + liquid-glass portfolio (previewed to Sean).** JVL previewed its own end-to-end **transactional product suite** (TITO, e-wallet, kiosk, POS card-dispensing, own merchant/processing) for the **July-1 Georgia gift-card** regime (statutory, no kiosk mandate; InComm serving 4,500+ locations), backward-compatible with dual- and single-SAS machines; and the next-gen **"liquid glass"** game portfolio (Fiery Ice Link, Jackpot Rush series, revamped Peak games). Rollout timeline in the summary reads beta/alpha ~May 2025 → full rollout end-2025 (dates as recorded; note the summary's dates trail the Feb-2026 meeting date). [[raw/Diamond_Shawn_Fellows_interview-February-9-2026.md]]

## Feb 10 2026 — transactional go-to-market: sell through the master, credit-note pricing

The day after the Diamond meeting, [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] and [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] worked out **how** to sell JVL's transactional-equipment line into Georgia ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-10-ga-coam-transactional-go-to-market-strategy.md|Feb-10 call]]), directly answering [[wiki/people/sean-fellows.md|Sean]]'s Feb-9 concern that a slot manufacturer can't go directly to locations. The model: **route through the master operators, never around them**, and give operators genuine financial incentive to participate. Full device/pricing detail on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md|the FlexPay business-architecture topic]]. A separate **written strategy document** synthesizes the Feb-9 Diamond meeting and this call. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]

- **Why through the master.** Operators tolerate InComm going directly to 4,500+ locations because they see InComm as a bank / financial-services company, not a gaming rival; a slot manufacturer "does not have that luxury." Because the systems connect to **COAMS**, master involvement is effectively mandatory anyway; Sean left open a **turnkey service to smaller staff-less operators, with the master's day-one approval**. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **The economic hooks.** POS retails ~$1,000, paid in full but offset by a **$1,000 credit note** against any JVL purchase (feels free, pulls repeat business); **$0.20 per card activation** paid to the master (also flushes out what InComm pays); **FlexPort** ~$1,000 with $700 credit (~$300 net); a stripped **FlexPay Lite** kiosk (screen + validator, no printer, phone QR codes); and a recycler-**ATM** path (progressing on Texas) that also enables InComm's coming gift-card-to-cash feature. Security (JVL integrates with the gaming system; InComm's handheld is manual/theft-prone) is the lead pitch; SAS-data-to-operators is offered with a **local-only or third-party-certified "Chinese wall"** to answer the "JVL knows too much" worry. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]

## Feb 12 2026 — revised-nudge RTP is the underperformance culprit; the go-to-market plan and the Felipe/office-manager question

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-12-game-development-review.md|Feb-12 review]] (segment 2; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]]). [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]

- **The revised-nudge RTP cut — the leading cause of the Feb-9 ~50% underperformance.** [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] showed that the most-deployed Georgia package ("1919") ran **all denominations at 92%**, whereas the revised-nudge package dropped **bet buttons 1/2/3 to 88%** (and cut buttons 4/5 by 2%) — a **4% cut at the most-played low bets, where all the coin-in happens**. Michael: "you have to feel that as a player… we're failing the $20 test" (the mid/low-tier Hispanic player comes in with $20–30 and burns through it faster). So the Feb-9 "only graphical, 50% drop doesn't seem logical" puzzle is explained: the change was **not** only graphical — the RTP fell. **Urgent fix**: revert the RIS to the 1919-style percentages (it "is a setting and a new RIS," startable Monday) so JVL can run an apples-to-apples test against the new nudge locations. Val: "the most urgent undertaking." [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]
- **Georgia machine-testing timeline.** Build relationships/locations and start **testing machines ~June 1**, run June–July, and come into the **August trade show** with positive results. For that, the **GCOM software must be ready last week of April / first week of May** to clear the **Eclipse** test lab for a June install; deliver most/many games first, get approved, add more. [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]
- **The 16-game "mega monster" vs a 10-game "Platinum" upgrade (Michael's push).** Michael worried a 16-game new-release "burns through a ton of titles for no justifiable reason"; he proposed bringing the count to **~10** (two Fiery Ice + two Jackpot Rush + the six Peach-style, lines-and-ways) and, separately, **upgrading the ~4-year-old Peach One base (1,000+ D27s in the field) to a new "Platinum" pack** (a couple of hold-and-respin + progressive tower + revised nudge + liquid glass) at an appropriate price — to keep JVL's box on the floor and block a bigger competitor. Val's caution: don't offer a **conversion kit** to operators ("the moment you say that, you'll never sell another machine" — it brands JVL a commodity). [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]
- **Commoditization worry.** Bad Dog refurbishes a "vanilla" cabinet with new content for ~$5–6k (50% cheaper than a new game); Puerto Rico shows Chinese CPU kits with knock-off Zeus/Fire Link at ~$1,500 in wooden cabinets — a race to the bottom. Georgia (mid-tier, Hispanic-player) is where JVL competes now until it re-enters top-tier locations; new markets (Nebraska/Texas/South Dakota) are pre-commoditization. [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]
- **The Felipe / office-manager / salesperson question.** Val and Juan debated the Georgia office: [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]]'s body language "isn't convincing" as a salesperson (Colombian, not the "good-old-boy" network, still fields ~20–30% of service calls). Two models floated (neither decided): (a) keep Felipe as **office manager / service** and hire a dedicated (white-American "hustler") salesperson; or (b) replace **[[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth]]** (weak English, timid, still consults Felipe) with an English-speaking office manager. A dedicated salesperson is hard/expensive to find — look at ex-**Vanilla**/distributor reps rather than the "prima donna" big-three (Aristocrat/IGT/L&W) refugees. [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]
- **A Konami/Bravery-Gaming idea (Michael, "insane ideas").** Partner with **Konami** (no Georgia distribution; their skill/nudge was botched by a since-fired video-game designer at Bravery Gaming) to run Konami content on JVL hardware for more location footprint — Val: "never thought about it, no opinion." Recorded as an unresolved brainstorm. [[raw/Game_Development-February-12-2026-2..md]]

## Feb 18 2026 — the nudge-RTP revert plan, big-master access, the Class-A threat

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-18-georgia-operators-nudge-revert-class-a.md|Feb-18 Georgia operators' to-do call]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]], 6:31 PM) the trio walked their undertakings from the recent operator meetings and set the plan to reverse the revised-nudge damage the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-12-game-development-review.md|Feb-12 review]] had diagnosed. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]

- **The RTP cut restated in numbers — 92 → 90 → 88.** [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] confirmed the newest nudge software is performing at **40–50% of the original Peach versions**; the change dropped the RTP setting on **bet buttons 1/2/3 by ~4%** (92% → 90% → 88%). Val stressed the magnitude: to a player, a 4% cut off a ~90% payout is felt as roughly a **35–40% reduction** in what they win back, so repeat players "burned" the product. He took the blame ("I pushed for that") and named his own mistake — when lowering the low bet to 88% he **should have raised the higher bet closer to 94%** and did not. Juan noted 88/89% on 1-and-2-cent denominations is a normal Class-3 casino setup (Seminole/Caesars/Harrah's reward the higher-denomination player), but agreed it hurts in a predominantly local, repeat-player market. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Change the game package, don't relocate the cabinet (the agreed fix).** Val's initial instinct was to move the burned machines to new locations; Juan resisted (operators won't relocate cabinets, and a new location muddies the reading). The group converged on **swapping the game package to an alternative on the same cabinet, set at the correct 92% RTP, and taking a reading in the same location** — because the same games at a merely-corrected percentage are "damaged in the eyes of the player," whereas a different-looking game gets a fresh shot. For older Peach-3 cabinets still on the old nudge (e.g. Allinson's, set at 88/90/92 with the new layout for the V-Deck / dual SAS), Val ruled it is not optional: any old-nudge Peach **must** be moved to the new nudge set at **92%**. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **The project: ~35 cabinets, 30–45 days, four operators first.** [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] has already listed the serial numbers of machines configured at 88/90/92%; the challenge is matching serials to physical locations (the lottery asset number ≠ the serial number). He emailed the four operators — **Inamax, Diamond Amusement, Allinson (Allison), and AKS** — the serial list, asking them to identify install locations and grant access to their head techs (reach the head techs at AKS/Allinson directly; go in person for Inamax/Diamond). Juan sized it a **30–45-day minimum** project (define locations → get RIS → have the customer install), ~**35 cabinets total** at roughly one cabinet a day, prioritising whichever of the four responds first. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Monitoring: QR scans vs cellular modems.** Some operators allow JVL to take QR scans in the locations, some do not; weekly monitoring is unrealistic, biweekly at best. Felipe proposed installing **cellular modems** to connect the machines to JVL's platform (JVL has done this before, including in Colombia) — Val agreed where operators permit, noting it reveals "hell of a lot more than the QR code," though it would not expose competitors' numbers (no pretext to enter for a competitor scan; at best a monthly photo). [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Sequencing into the next product wave.** The 30–45-day revert carries JVL "through the end of March," near when it can submit the newest **Fiery Ice Link** (transcribed "fiery eyes"), **Jackpot Rush**, and enhanced Peach software. Juan doubts pushing **Diamond Link** against Aristocrat / Light & Wonder — the non-deluxe Diamond Link "never really stood out," under-performing even Peach Vertical, and (per Felipe) it hasn't caught on because Georgia games spread by operators sharing photos/word-of-mouth and Diamond Link isn't being asked for. Business is "very slow right now." [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Big-master access is the real constraint (Felipe's market map).** Felipe laid out the top masters: **United Gaming** (#1, the largest — ~1,500+ locations; Felipe cited a large approved-decal count, exact figure garbled), **Epic Amusement** (Kenny; ~520 locations, difficult, promising an order since November), **Lucky Bucks / Arc Luck** (~350 locations), **Bulldog** (~240 locations; won't buy until JVL fixes a freezing issue — JVL gave them five machines), and **Ultra** — the top five ≈ **14,500 machines across ~2,800 locations** ("they call the shots"). The whole market is ~**45,000 machines / ~6,500 locations**, with ~198 master *licensees* but really only ~**60–70 distinct owners** (some hold several master licenses); ~117 masters have <50 locations and ~64 have <20. JVL needs the **top ~20**. United's hang-up is JVL's **performance vs competitors**: Felipe can reach only **Jason Hall** (director of operations), not **Ashish** (older, buys what he's told, family-bonded with owner **Bunty**); United has bought ~200 cabinets from JVL directly but acquired many more JVL machines by buying up routes, which may be dampening direct orders. **Adam Faust**, United's ex-CFO, now runs **Skillmaster Pro** (an Aristocrat distributor) and is close to Bunty. Juan's conclusion: JVL likely has to wait for a **performance turnaround** before it can re-approach United. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Facebook / geo-marketing idea (Val).** To reach players indirectly, Val proposed **geo-targeted Facebook/Instagram advertising** of JVL's games/machines ("ask your location to have it for you"), possibly letting players **play the games for free** on Facebook (JVL has a mechanism to port them quickly). He has technical staff strong at Facebook/Google ad optimisation (they do it successfully for funeral homes) and put it on his own undertakings. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Aircore / Echo review — probably not worth it yet.** Val questioned whether the **Echo** program is still needed. Only ~**10 Echo COAM games** are in the field (Ultra bought one, Peachtree a few; not all active), so the work to make Echo support TITO may not be justified — and **TITO is dying** (operators want digital wallet / QR, not TITO; Diamond's Sean Fellows is trying Axis, TITO, Easylink and Wynn at once). Any Echo solution must drop the cable daisy-chain (the connection is the problem). Georgia is >90% gas-station/convenience stores with no counter for a bar-top Echo. The **July 1 credits-to-card deadline** may bring more corporate/bar locations on board (no in-store redemption needed → acceptable there). Cross-ref [[wiki/topics/echo-home-gaming-device.md]]. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **The Class-A machine threat (Felipe's GAMOA board briefing).** At a GAMOA board-of-directors meeting (JVL a sponsor), Felipe learned that **Primero** (and reportedly Ivy Production/Promotions and Vanilla — three manufacturers, Primero leading) is bringing out **Class-A "amusement" machines** that look like Class-B COAMs but behave differently: **two separate meters** (money-in and cash-out), so $20 buys 20 fixed credits regardless of wins, winnings accrue to a separate cash-out (gift-card/kiosk) meter, and the **max win is $50 per spin**. They sell for ~**$3,000–4,000**, need **no master license** (no ~$1.4M master licence; unlimited licensees), and can sit next to Class-B machines indistinguishably — with a proposed large **progressive** (e.g. $50k) that pays out as a race of $50-per-push. The board fears these **cannibalise Class-B COAM locations**; only the **GLC/lottery** could regulate or tax them but (as with the kiosk) has signalled it won't, and a court fight would run years. **JVL's response (Val and Juan agreed):** offer JVL's **countertop / Echo units as Class-A machines** (paired with JVL's transactional gift-card product), explicitly **not sold to Class-B customers** so it isn't a competing product, and stop if regulators ever move — "if it's legal, why wouldn't we?" (Val recalled JVL selling ~1,000 countertops/month to truck stops 20 years ago). Val doesn't fully understand the Class-A mechanics/RTP yet and wants a separate meeting and more information. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Transactional-product showing (March).** Val judged JVL's transactional product close enough to start showing in March — but as a **private "friends and family," one-on-one / wine-and-cheese** series at the Georgia office, not a large audience. The showing would include the **POS**, the **kiosk** (current form plus a version with the **Fujitsu dispenser** — mainly for Texas and Nebraska — and a separate recycler model). Separately, **Rich (Richard)** is to attempt operator conversions this week (Adam in the Georgia office on phone support); if he can't or won't, JVL sends someone. Cross-ref [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]]. [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]

## Feb 19 2026 — Georgia Vertical 2 game-selection screen; the 8-vs-10-game debate; layout flexibility

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-19-games-design-meeting.md|Feb-19 Games Design Meeting]] (game-review session; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]]) the team reviewed the Georgia Vertical 2 game-selection screen and debated how many games to ship. [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_review_feb19_2026_v3.md]]

- **The game-selection screen — current layout = 10 games.** **6 Lines & Ways + 2 Jackpot Rush + 2 Fiery Ice Link.** L&W: Rodeo Queen, Rodeo Spins, Bayou Riches, Queen of Luxor, plus **Dragon Dreams → Twin Bison** and **Gypsy Spirit → Lion's Riches** (the two swaps land as those games complete concept). Jackpot Rush: Sparkle 7, Sapphire Star. Fiery Ice Link: Kyocho (Calle Ocho), Rodeo Queen. [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_review_feb19_2026_v3.md]]
- **Fiery Ice Link category relabel.** The Fiery Ice Link section header will read **"Hold and Re-Spin Games with Progressive"** — Lightning Link and Dragon Link have been in the market 10+ years, so the term is well understood and immediately tells the player the mechanic. [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]]'s market data is required to validate/refine the label before final regulatory submission. Michael also floated descriptive text for the Jackpot Rush section ("Collect Jackpot Rush symbols to win progressives") — not decided. [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_open_items_feb19_2026.md]]
- **The 8-vs-10-game debate (deferred to Juan; Val on record disagreeing).** Val recommended an **8-game standard package** (4 L&W + 2 Jackpot Rush + 2 Fiery Ice Link); [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] favored **10 games** for the initial select-customer deployment, framing the release as a **data-collection exercise** (more titles = more comparative performance data; after 90 days he would disable the two underperformers to make the box "more efficient"). Val's counter: games cost **~$50k each** to produce (burn them strategically); four of the ten are Peach-vertical games customers already own (so the 8-pack carries more genuinely-new titles); and dropping games after a test is impractical and creates bad faith in a **tight-knit ~25-operator** circuit — "you're going to end up giving everybody 10 games." Juan: Georgia makes software updates hard (JVL can't touch machines at will), so maximize data per deployment; "a good game is a good game no matter where you put it." Val ended it with his standing division of authority: **"Michael should decide how the games are designed, and you [Juan] should decide how the games go out"** — and deferred the go-to-market count to Juan. [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_review_feb19_2026_v3.md]]
- **Val's premium-pricing model (proposed, unresolved).** Sell the **8-game pack at a base price** (e.g. a $14,000 machine) and offer additional games at a **premium** (~$500/game, ~$2,000 for four extra), with the base machine run on limited-time sale/promotion; give test-customers the extra games as a perceived credit/discount rather than free. Juan was skeptical JVL can charge for software again until the new product proves it makes money ("what we tried to charge for software did not go so well… we need to earn our way back"); Val: "if our software was making money, people would pay for it." [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_open_items_feb19_2026.md]]
- **Flexible-layout requirement (Val).** The game-selection screen must support multiple configurations **without redesign or regulatory re-approval**: **Lines & Ways 4–8, Jackpot Rush 2–4, Fiery Ice Link 2–4**, with icons that **auto-center/reflow** when titles are enabled or disabled per location via RIS, and Jackpot Rush / Fiery Ice kept close to the player (below L&W). [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]] confirmed multiple layouts are already prepared as standard practice. (Captured as REQ-DRAFT-4 in the meeting's derived report.) [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_review_feb19_2026_v3.md]]
- **Revised-nudge RTP — Val's on-record accountability (continuing [[#feb-12-2026--revised-nudge-rtp-is-the-underperformance-culprit-the-go-to-market-plan-and-the-felipeoffice-manager-question|Feb-12]] / [[#feb-18-2026--the-nudge-rtp-revert-plan-big-master-access-the-class-a-threat|Feb-18]]).** Val again took **personal responsibility** for the new-nudge field underperformance — root cause the **88% RTP on the lowest bet button**, a setup he directed — and stated that with the corrected **92% floor** for new deployments he expects **at least a 20% performance improvement over the previous average** in new locations. The nudge implementation itself is excellent; "we screwed up… the fault is with the setup." [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]]
- **Long-term Georgia strategy (discussion only, no decisions).** The initial Vertical 2 release (August) is framed as a **data-gathering** exercise. Future ideas raised: a **Peach 1/2/3 D27 refresh** with the match-3 progressive + liquid glass + enhanced nudge; **Jackpot Rush / Fiery Ice D27** versions (Aristocrat runs both a vertical and a dual-screen "Baozu Festival" in Georgia); and a **Georgia Vertical 3** that takes the top Vertical-2 performers, adds new titles, and replaces the bottom performers. Efim also confirmed the progressive triggering decisions are **product requirements, not ADRs** — they originate from Juan/Felipe market observations and are translated into JVL's technical answer by Michael and Efim. [[raw/Game_Development_transcript_game_review-February-19-26.md]] [[raw/Games_Design_Meeting_review_feb19_2026_v3.md]]

## Feb 27 2026 — Georgia 8-pack base game confirmed; August hard date, June realistic completion

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-27-game-development-review.md|Feb-27 game-development review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]]) the Georgia go-to-market advanced while Nebraska took priority (Nebraska detail on [[wiki/projects/nebraska-skill-market.md]]; the condensed pay-table design mechanic on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]], both Feb-27). [[raw/Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]] [[raw/Summary_Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]]

- **The 8-pack base game configuration (Georgia).** The base game is an **8-pack**: **2 Jackpot Rush + 2 Fiery Ice + 4 Lines & Ways** games default-enabled, with **6 Lines & Ways titles** submitted so the operator can toggle among them; JVL is building **software packages** where wanting **10 or 12** games is a different (tiered) price for the customer. Of the L&W set, Michael expects a hard internal choice among six strong titles (Rodeo Spins is a Crazy-Spins clone / the #1 performer; Bayou Riches likely swapped for the top-performing **Lion's Riches**; possibly Queen of Luxor for Twin Bison) — Juan to finalize by feature. This continues the [[#feb-19-2026--georgia-vertical-2-game-selection-screen-the-8-vs-10-game-debate-layout-flexibility|Feb-19 8-vs-10-game debate]] (still Juan's call). [[raw/Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]] [[raw/Summary_Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]]
- **Georgia timeline — hard August date, June realistic.** Juan has a **hard date** for Georgia product to be **viewable by the 2nd week of August** at the annual gaming show; working back, he wanted it lab-ready by **end of March / early-to-late April**, but Lena called mid-April "absolutely impossible" and Val/Lena landed on a **realistic completion of mid-May to June** (lab submission by end of May; the lab needs ~30–45 days given the significant changes). The date isn't treated as slipped — the August show is fixed, the internal completion moved. [[raw/Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]] [[raw/Summary_Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]]
- **Nebraska liquid glass deferred to protect Georgia.** Val pushed to disregard the Nebraska **GC372 liquid-glass** work (deleted, see [[wiki/projects/nebraska-skill-market.md]] Feb-27) partly because a ~2-month liquid-glass effort **would delay Georgia** (Lena's urgent priority is to finish South Dakota, then immediately start Georgia); liquid glass for both markets can be revisited in summer, after Georgia ships. [[raw/Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]] [[raw/Summary_Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]]
- **Condensed pay-table layout for the two new Georgia flagships.** Lion's Riches and Twin Bison adopt the new **symbol-pay-row + big-character-canvas** screen (a massive lion / two bison instead of a full pay table) — kept to those two new games; existing games keep their pay tables. Design detail on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Feb-27). [[raw/Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]] [[raw/Summary_Game_Development-February-27-2026.md]]

## Mar 13 2026 — Georgia pick-3 progressive designs; casino royals; Crazy Colt; COAM backlog item

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-13-game-development-review.md|Mar-13 game-development review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]]) the Georgia content pipeline was walked in the JIRA schedule review; the design mechanics (condensed pay table, casino-vs-skill royals) are on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Mar-13). [[raw/JIRA_Schedule_review-March-13-2026.md]] [[raw/Game_Development-March-13-2026.md]]

- **Georgia pick-3 progressive items in flight.** **GC351** — a **pick-three, three-level progressive** for Georgia — is in coin/coin-layout design ([[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael]] with [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]]); **GC360** is the progressive visual for the "Pix 3" bonus, **in progress**; **GC364** is the **Lion's Riches** update, with **GC366** carrying the simplified pay-table + big-lion canvas (abstract due Monday March 16). [[raw/JIRA_Schedule_review-March-13-2026.md]]
- **Casino-royals move for the Georgia flagships.** The Georgia games swap **abstract royals for casino-type royals** ("skill royals vs casino royals"); Val judged **Crazy Colt** (for Georgia; first introduction, no field data yet) the strongest of the visuals — "looks right… going to kill it" — and floated it as a **vertical** game reusable in **Nebraska, South Dakota and Texas** verticals ([[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]]: Nebraska-vertical and South-Dakota-vertical are the next topics). Design detail on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Mar-13). [[raw/Game_Development-March-13-2026.md]]
- **IP1028 Georgia COAM sits in backlog.** [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]] created **IP1028 (Georgia COAM)** as a placeholder for the next design-stage work but has **not scheduled it** yet — it moves to design as the pick-3 / line-riches concepts finish. Val confirmed it is correctly parked in backlog. [[raw/JIRA_Schedule_review-March-13-2026.md]]
- **Closing priority.** With Nebraska-2 done, the team's stated focus returns to **South Dakota, then Georgia** — consistent with the [[#feb-27-2026--georgia-8-pack-base-game-confirmed-august-hard-date-june-realistic-completion|Feb-27 August-show hard date / June-realistic]] plan. [[raw/Game_Development-March-13-2026.md]]

## Mar 20 2026 — the pick-3 level-progressive presentation (GC351/GC359/GC360)

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-20-game-development-schedule-review.md|Mar-20 game-development schedule review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]]) the team reviewed the presentation art for the **Georgia pick-3 level progressive**. [[raw/JIRA_schedule_review_Game_Development-March-20-2026.md]]

- **The pick-3 progressive screen (IP535 / GC351, presented via GC359 + GC360).** GC351 is described as the **"pick 33" level progressive for Georgia**; GC359/GC360 are how it will look. Design language: **color-coded "liquid glass"** progressive meters, a subtle animated background bringing the meters forward, and a **prominent coin-flying-to-the-proper-slot** special effect (with a substantial win effect when you get three). Michael and Lena said they were happy with it after tweaks; Val approved — "clean and comprehendible… no comments." (Visual-design detail on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]], Mar-20.) [[raw/JIRA_schedule_review_Game_Development-March-20-2026.md]]
- **The selling point = a guaranteed progressive.** Michael stressed "progressive" deliberately: in the old model you would pick from the bin or the progressive hid in the peach bonus and you often just got a **credit value**; here **you are guaranteed to land one of the progressives**. The grand is **scripted** — it will come up once or twice to build anticipation even though the player can't actually win it. [[raw/JIRA_schedule_review_Game_Development-March-20-2026.md]]

## Georgia-operator briefings (2026)

Alongside the Feb-9 Diamond partnership, JVL prepared a set of **operator-profile briefings** for its 2026 Georgia meeting cycle — pre-meeting reference documents on the state's larger COAM masters. Three are captured here, each with its own org page: [[wiki/organizations/aks-amusement.md|AKS Amusement]], [[wiki/organizations/diamond-amusement.md|Diamond Amusement]], and [[wiki/organizations/inamax-ultra.md|Inamax / Ultra]]. Together they give the clearest cross-operator read of the master landscape and JVL's footprint within it. [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_AKS.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Diamond_Amusement.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Inamax_Ultra.md]]

- **Master landscape (2026 decal rankings).** Georgia's COAM market is ranked by decals assigned. The three profiled masters: **Inamax / Ultra — 1,140 decals, ~228 locations, ~#5 statewide** (large); **Diamond Amusement — 1,002 decals, ~200 locations, ~#6** (large); **AKS Amusement — 510 decals, ~102 locations, ~#18** (medium–large). Location counts are briefing estimates using the ~5-machines-per-location typical GA configuration. Inamax is the most operationally sophisticated group and a "reference operator" (what it standardizes on tends to spread to smaller operators); Diamond is highly influential (its CEO, Shawn/Sean Fellows, is a two-term ex-GAMOA President). [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Inamax_Ultra.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Diamond_Amusement.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_AKS.md]]
- **JVL Flex deployments (direct purchases).** All three run JVL Flex cabinets: **AKS — 29 Flex D27 + 9 Flex V43** (the latest V43 buy tied to a trade-in); **Inamax — 26 Flex D27 + 8 Flex V43 + 1 Echo COAM**; **Diamond — 18 Flex D27 + 6 Flex V43** (recent). [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_AKS.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Inamax_Ultra.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Diamond_Amusement.md]]
- **Indirect / legacy exposure and Altera-R.** Beyond direct sales, JVL's footprint grows through **route acquisitions and cabinet transfers**. Diamond controls multiple **legacy JVL units** from route purchases (footprint growing via acquisitions since 2024). **AKS** holds **Altera-R units and additional Flex cabinets acquired indirectly** through cabinet transfers and split-order distribution from other masters (e.g. Dynamic Games LLC orders split across AKS and Dynamic); AKS's briefing also ties it to the Lucky Bucks-adjacent smaller-master ecosystem. **Inamax** does **not** appear to hold significant additional JVL units via third-party / route acquisitions (briefing flags this as unconfirmed and worth validating). [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Diamond_Amusement.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_AKS.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Inamax_Ultra.md]]
- **The upgrade-for-data / nudge thread.** After AKS's trade-in, [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] pitched the newest software package (including the newly designed **Nudge feature**) to AKS CEO Zahir and agreed **in principle to a no-charge software upgrade conditional on AKS supplying location-level Georgia Lottery performance reports** for all machines. This upgrade-for-data structure connects directly to the **Feb-18 plan (above)** to match machine serial numbers and drive the game-package swap + 92%-RTP revert across the **four operators (Inamax, Diamond, Allinson, AKS)**. [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_AKS.md]] [[raw/To-Do_Georgia_meetings-February-18-2026.md]]
- **Nebraska cross-jurisdiction (Inamax).** Inamax CFO Ray Cosentino is interested in **testing JVL units in Nebraska under Inamax's Nebraska skill software**; Felipe has already introduced Ray to [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] by email to keep that trial coordination moving — a parallel to the broader Nebraska-vertical work on this page. [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Inamax_Ultra.md]]
- **Relationship / support burden.** JVL's relationships across the three are positive; support load tracks operator maturity — **AKS ~70–90 inbound service calls (2025–26)**, **Diamond ~60–80 (2025–26)**, **Inamax ~10 (2024–25, mostly hard resets)**, the last a notably low burden (JVL also sent technician Andres to Inamax's warehouse twice in 2024 for CPU-board fixes). The key open executive item is the **Inamax constraint** — why its JVL purchasing stays limited relative to its large footprint despite no performance complaints, gated by ops-manager Ashley Gunther's product approval (tracked on the [[wiki/organizations/inamax-ultra.md|Inamax / Ultra]] page). [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_AKS.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Diamond_Amusement.md]] [[raw/georgia_operator_briefing_Inamax_Ultra.md]]

## Sept 2 2026 — a direct VPN link into Georgia, and the Georgia-specific constraints on the Flex-IQ pilot

Three Georgia-specific items came out of the [[wiki/meetings/2026-09-02-flex-iq-pre-pilot-updates-after-the-show.md|Sept-2 Flex-IQ pre-pilot call]] (Russian). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Barcode_Receipt_Ewallet_Strategy-September-2-2026.md]]

- **A direct VPN channel from Georgia into the team's office has just been initiated.** [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]]: "мы сейчас инициировали прямой VPN канал из [Джорджии] к нам сюда в офис, чтоб мы могли легко к вашему оборудованию подключаться" — so the switcher work on the new boards can be done remotely, possibly against a bench set up in Georgia. This is the same remote-work problem the team hit on the CPI recycler a week earlier (Aug-26, above), now with a concrete link. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Barcode_Receipt_Ewallet_Strategy-September-2-2026.md]]
- **The Flex-IQ wording pass removes terms best not used in the market.** [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei]]: "частичные изменения по вордингам[,] там были местами ошибки упоминания терминов, которые в go лучше не упоминать" — cosmetic, but "её лучше доделать, чтобы смело заходить на локацию." *(The transcript renders the place as "go"; the derived note reads it as Georgia — recorded as transcribed, not asserted.)* See [[wiki/decisions/2026-09-02-implicit-consent-and-mandatory-printed-receipt.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Barcode_Receipt_Ewallet_Strategy-September-2-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_IQ_Barcode_Receipt_Ewallet_Strategy-September-2-2026.md]]
- **A hypothesis against the phone-based wallet, specific to Georgia players.** Andrei: "есть гипотеза[,] то, что игроки. Конкретно в штате Джорджия не очень расположены к использованию своего смартфона для игр по разным причинам." Val's response was to record that nobody knows, and to keep moving on printed carriers meanwhile. Detail on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Sept-2). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Barcode_Receipt_Ewallet_Strategy-September-2-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear. **§15 #9:** clear.

## Mar 27 2026 — Transactional go-to-market: Georgia among the three named markets

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-27-alignment-andrei-klimovich.md|Mar-27 alignment]] Val named **Georgia, Texas and Nebraska** as the three "hot" markets for JVL's **Transactional equipment** line, and put [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] (starting **April 2, 2026** as Product & Market Manager — Transactional) on the market-facing side of it. For **Georgia specifically**, Val said JVL already knows **~80% of the most important customers** (Texas is largely unknown; Nebraska is a couple of small operators), and that in Georgia the buyer is a **dual customer — location and operator** — sold through channel partner [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Philippe/Felipe]] rather than by Andrei directly. Andrei's job there is corporate-figure relationship-building and gathering product requirements, not selling. The first Transactional shipment is targeted to **Texas ~June 1, 2026**, not Georgia. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Andrei_Klimovich-2-March-27-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_PMM_Appointment-March-27-2026.md]]

## Mar 31 2026 — Felipe sales strategy: market-sizing, freezing-fix reputation play, Texas/Nebraska skill

On a ~27-minute one-on-one ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-31-felipe-credit-terms-sales-strategy.md|Mar-31 call]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] and [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]]) Felipe worried that a new game for the existing vertical cabinet would land with operators as "just an upgrade" on machines they already own; Val reframed the opportunity as selling **whole machines into the un-covered market**. (The credit-terms half of the same call is on [[wiki/topics/jvl-finance-back-office.md]].) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Felipe_credit_terms_sales_strategy-March-31-2026.md]]

- **Market-sizing pitch.** JVL has ~**2,100 machines** installed, at best ~**1 per location** ≈ ~**30%** coverage of the addressable base; the other ~**70% (~4,000 locations)** have no JVL machine. Covering 30% of those 4,000 = ~**1,200 machines** ≈ **two years of sales** at Felipe's best year of ~**600 machines/year**. Vertical ceiling is higher still (fewer verticals sold to date; operators may take both vertical and horizontal). Val's price argument to operators: a JVL machine at ~**$13,000** vs ~**$22,000 (IGT)** / ~**$25,000 (Light & Wonder)**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Felipe_credit_terms_sales_strategy-March-31-2026.md]]
- **The freezing-fix reputation play.** Val attributes the win-per-day shortfall to machines being **out of commission ~30–50% of the time** from the freeze/reset bug — now fixed (the Linux-kernel fix + second watchdog, see [[wiki/topics/omni-core-advancement-and-game-templating.md]], the full [[wiki/topics/egm-freezing-investigation.md]], and the [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-30-black-horse-invoice-payment-review.md|Mar-30 review]]). Plan: release a **"we found the problem"** message; frame the recovered downtime as turning a machine into a **stellar performer** (not a math or game problem, a fixed reset issue); and offer loyal customers **free software swaps on their existing machines**, monitored via **QR codes** to demonstrate pre/post performance so the economics sell themselves. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Felipe_credit_terms_sales_strategy-March-31-2026.md]]
- **Skill markets open a parallel sales line.** JVL now has the **Nebraska/Texas "full skill"** product it lacked a year ago, so Georgia operators expanding into other markets — who previously could not choose JVL — now can. The **first Texas machine netted $280/day win** (data delivered by [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] that day); it runs the **"previous version" of skill**, aligned to **Light & Wonder's less-binding "initial skill feature"** methodology rather than the original Vanilla-inspired feature ("notch"). Val expects later machines to perform considerably better and told Felipe to tell operators expanding into new markets that JVL is now an option. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Felipe_credit_terms_sales_strategy-March-31-2026.md]]
- **Nebraska rollout status + no geographic restriction.** New **Nebraska software** was submitted to the state enforcement authority ~a week earlier (Nebraska has no gaming commission — an alcohol/enforcement commission handles it; approval typically ~2 weeks); operators are lined up, machines are in place, and the **RIS was issued to [[wiki/people/denis-desmarais.md|Dennis]]**. Val told Felipe to **install the new Nebraska software now at Inamax for in-house testing** (get it from Dennis) and confirmed **no geographic restriction on Felipe's sales** — Georgia-sold machines can go to Inamax or any market. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Felipe_credit_terms_sales_strategy-March-31-2026.md]]

## Apr 21 2026 — Transactional competitive-landscape discovery + the POS-first product direction

A session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-21-transactional-financial-model.md|Transactional Financial Model / Competitive Landscape]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) scheduled as a pricing-strategy meeting but which Val pivoted in the first two minutes: he judged discussing pricing before mapping the competitive landscape "shooting ourselves in the foot," and the group unanimously redirected to cataloguing competitors, their capabilities and their pricing. Pricing strategy deferred to the next meeting (moved to **Wed Apr 29** to give Andrei research time). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]

- **Competitor inventory (Georgia focus).** Named: **[[wiki/organizations/incom.md|Incomm]]** (dominant transactional processor, distributes the **Pax 80** handheld); **Gewete / GWT** (Austrian, part of Germany's Merkur group — the **Count R** kiosk, deployed in Nebraska); **SGK** (Texas-based, full ecosystem with published pricing); **Vanilla Games / Cadrex** (Cadrex a hardware maker); **EasyLynx** (SaaS/TITO provider); **Intuitive Financial Systems** (gift-card dispenser, distributed by **Ivy Promotions** / Fred Ivy — reportedly being pulled from locations on uptime); IGT's **M3T** kiosk (also pulled, reputedly poor Georgia service); **Win Systems / Winners Marketing**; **Banilla**; **Access** (AFT + printer bundle); **Arc** (Dean's company — Mark Martinez pitching a Mother-Goose + back-end-TITO dual-print integration via dual SAS + shared bill-acceptor signal); **Bulldogs Gaming Kiosk / Ultra / Inna Max** (proprietary operators staying on their own solutions); **Blue Scale Kiosk** (Felipe's read on the white-labeled solution behind Mims Amusements' gift-card→player-card transfer); **Billy Luke** (phone-to-bank digital-wallet pitch — *read on Sept 2 2026 as [[wiki/people/billy-liu.md|Billy Liu]] of [[wiki/organizations/play-payments.md|Play Payments]]; an inference, see the Sept-2 evening section below*); **Ready Payouts** (cash-to-card self-service). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Pricing intel captured.** SGK's website: **kiosk $7K, printer $169, link board $200, "redemption tablet" $700** (the tablet functionally like a Pax 80), plus a free root-management app and mobile app; coverage TX/PR/VA/GA/NC/WI. JVL's records show the **Pax 80 at $800 or $1,000** — Juan believes that's legacy/high ("a glorified Android device") and owns the double-check. Val's separate read: Incomm's actual Pax cost ~$250 with a ~$300 sell — i.e. Incomm runs the hardware **at a loss** to secure the transactional relationship, because it is a transactional processor (Val: "a $6 billion processing company"), not a hardware house. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Incomm's posture + the fraud problem.** Pax is fully manual (an employee keys the payout amount and encodes a blank card), creating heavy shrinkage — Val/Juan recalled Incomm citing **$20K–$30K/day** of theft at one point. Val's thesis (endorsed by Juan): Incomm doesn't want the Pax business and has signalled it will yield the POS position to JVL as its contracts expire, provided JVL delivers a secure **dispenser-based POS** that locks cards and reads the win signal directly from the machine (intercepting between Mother Goose and the printer, reducing the operator's choice to a single yes/no confirmation) — eliminating an estimated **90–95% of fraud**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]
- **The double-conversion legal flow (Georgia).** Winnings move **e-wallet → prize card (dispensed) → skill card → ATM-redeemable cash**. Incomm has told JVL that GLC has approved a forthcoming card variant redeemable at any ATM for a service fee (**$2.50–$5** per transaction, believed up to a $1,000 cap — Felipe challenged this, noting ATMs typically cap ~$200/withdrawal), expected live **~September**. Val/Juan judged this would "kill everyone else" not adopting it. Val's rhetorical point: the player experiences win-to-cash in ~5 seconds ("within one millisecond") even though legally the money passes through every step virtually. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Product direction — POS → dispenser → recycler (FlexSlim).** A phased evolution: (1) **POS alone** already removes most manual-entry fraud (the win amount comes from the machine signal); (2) **POS + dispenser** (~500 preloaded cards locked in the unit); (3) **POS + recycler (FlexSlim)** — the ultimate target, because recycling needs no cash-refill logistics (contrast Texas/Nebraska dispenser-only setups needing armored-truck refills — "incredibly expensive"). Val floated a long-term vision of machines built with an integrated recycler (parallel to the 1998 bill-acceptor "dog house" era) and expects recyclers below $1,000 at volume. FlexSlim first production units target **June 20** (a shipment leaving Mexico City the next day per Juan); alpha ~**May 1** (HFM 513 production May 15), staying in the Georgia office (3D-printed plastic, two sets, internal QA only). Device roadmap on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Why POS-first — the retrofit economics.** Val did the math live: ~**6,500 locations × $7,000/kiosk ≈ $45M**, plus ~**35,000 machines × $1,000** for a bill-acceptor + printer + install **≈ $35M** — **~$80M total pre-labor**; and ~20% of current inventory (5,000–7,000 machines) has only a single SAS port already occupied by the Lottery Corporation and **cannot be retrofitted at all**. Val/Juan framed this as a non-starter for masters ("a 10-year project," done in-field at gas stations with custom harnesses), which is exactly why JVL "refocused away from a payment kiosk to a POS — a no-brainer." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Market urgency + Andrei's Georgia trip.** Felipe reported masters calling with genuine urgency over the last two weeks ahead of the **July 1** enforcement, with no single solution yet embraced. The group settled Andrei arriving **~May 13 for the May 14 meeting** (not May 1); the alpha stays in-office and the trip is customer relationship-building/market familiarization, not a product demo. Andrei's competitor-research undertaking and trip logistics are tracked on [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]. **§15.8:** Val also gave demo-sequencing coaching keyed to ethnic sub-communities in the operator market — acknowledged on record as ethically fraught and flagged for HR-sanitization; not restated here. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_transactional_financial_model-April-21-2026.md]]

## May 4 2026 — July trial rollout plan: ~10 free V43 machines into top locations ahead of the Aug 15 show

A late-evening call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-04-july-trial-rollout-plan.md|July Trial Rollout Plan]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]]) planning a Georgia field trial timed to produce live performance data before the **August 15** gaming show. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_july_trial_rollout_plan-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]

- **The core proposal.** Seed ~**10 free complete V43 machines** into hand-picked operator locations; operators keep all revenue for a **3–6 month** trial, then buy. **JVL chooses the locations** — only the top 5–10% qualify, because an average location produces misleading data and damages the product story. Included in the deal: a no-charge upgrade to the final software (targeted August), which Val wants institutionalised as a **6–12-month software warranty** bundled with a machine purchase. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_july_trial_rollout_plan-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Val's placement math.** The metric isn't "how many machines" but "how many top-tier locations we can get into." Rule of thumb: a 50-location operator has ~5 strong locations (top 10%) and one exceptional one; the rest "fall off a cliff." A list of **10 mid-size operators** (50+ locations each) yields ~20 candidate top locations, of which ~10 machines can be placed across **≥8 operators**. Felipe judged he can credibly push 5–8 masters into top-location placement; a technician "kicker" can grease installs. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_july_trial_rollout_plan-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Primary offer vs. fallback + the "swap."** The primary push is an **incremental free machine**; a **software upgrade** is the fallback for operators whose premium location already runs a JVL machine and can't take a second unit. Where a swap is possible, Val's logic: the operator moves their owned V43 to their second-best location and JVL's free machine takes the prime spot ("nothing beats free"). Felipe cited Allenson/Malik as the model — a location that rose from ~$320 to ~$600–700/day hold after an upgrade. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_july_trial_rollout_plan-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]
- **The Aug 15 show.** 10 machines shipped ~July 1 gives ~45 days of field data going into the show, converting Felipe's pitch from renders to real revenue numbers. Booth 215 (20x20, ~$7,500 gross / ~$9,000 with banner, sponsor discount) stays — the show is small ("walk it in 30 minutes") so location matters more than size; upsize to booth 115 (~$10,000) parked. Product mix: **V43 + D27** (Val kept D27 over Juan's question, with adjusted proportions), plus e-wallet/Tito/FlexPay/POS/recycler/dispenser and **two premium games** on top of those already tested. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_july_trial_rollout_plan-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Texas Maples.** Val told Felipe to hold off actively pitching the Texas Maples for a few days pending a **new QR-code-reader integration**, but is confident the games will perform. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_july_trial_rollout_plan-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]
- **May 5 post-meeting update (from the notes).** Felipe delivered the target list — 10 priority operators (Diamond Amusements, Ultra/Inamax, MSII Legends, Five Stars, Jiya Game, AKS, Prestige, Gzone, Atlanta Game World, Everest) + 5 contingency; Val approved it and moved the target ship date from **July 1 to the last week of June 2026** (the July 4 long weekend would compress the pre-show window). Felipe owns securing operator commitments + a brief signed agreement per operator and building the rollout schedule over the following ~2 weeks (~May 19). §15.8: market/machine/booth economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_July_Trial_Rollout_Plan-May-4-2026.md]]

## May 4 2026 — GA-V2 schedule review: the August show is the real target, not a July field deployment

A working review of Lena's Jira schedule for the **GA-V2 (Georgia Vertical 2 / Liquid Glass) release** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-04-ga-v2-schedule-review.md|GA-V2 Schedule Review]]; notes-only; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena Zhukova]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]]). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_V2_Schedule_Review-May-4-2026.md]]

- **The bottleneck is graphic assets, not integration.** Val filtered the board and showed the integration developers (Anton, Igor) aren't yet scheduled on Liquid Glass — the detailed schedule so far covers only Liquid Glass graphics (Dmitriy Khandov + artists), because the programmer-integration portion hasn't been built yet. Val agreed integration is not the GA-V2 bottleneck. He probed whether the strongest animal animator (Anastasia), allocated to Twin Bison for the August show, was being held back from GA-V2; Lena pushed back that Bison and Lions Riches are the flagship show pieces and Anastasia must start now. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_V2_Schedule_Review-May-4-2026.md]]
- **The realignment — August, not July.** The deeper question surfaced: does GA-V2 need a mid-June release at all? Juan and Michael confirmed the **August 15 show was always the authoritative target**; a July field deployment to Felipe is unlikely to yield meaningful data (Felipe consumed with the watchdog-timer rollout — an issue found that day, root cause unknown; gift cards live July 1; the July 4 holiday; slow Georgia operators). **No formal GA-V2 release date was committed** — deferred until Lena delivers the full detailed schedule (including programmer integration) on May 5. Anastasia stays on Twin Bison pending the May 5 planning session. Val warned that without a live field test the August product carries elevated risk. The resourcing trade-off against South Dakota work touches [[wiki/projects/south-dakota-vlt-market.md]]. §15.8: schedule/resourcing/game-build economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_V2_Schedule_Review-May-4-2026.md]]

## May 8 2026 — GA-COAM v2 capability decomposition + requirements workflow (two alignment sessions)

Two back-to-back May-8 sessions ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-08-ga-coam-v2-capability-decomposition.md|Capability Decomposition]], 1:11pm ET; and the reconvened [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-08-ga-coam-v2-requirements-workflow-personas.md|Requirements Workflow and Personas]], 3:00pm ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim Podvoiski]], [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor Metelski]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) formalising the **V2 / V43 GA-COAM v2 "3-JP-Progressive"** release into capabilities, requirements, features and personas. The capability/requirement/feature/persona *method* is documented on [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]] (May-8); the GA-COAM product substance is below. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_capability_decomposition-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_requirements_workflow_and_personas-May-8-2026.md]]

- **The product is three capabilities, not one.** Juan's single "Capability 9" ("deliver a new jackpot experience to replace the Georgia peach progressive bonus; 3 levels of progressive; match-3 symbol concept; compatible with all legacy line-and-ways games in a multi-game set") was split into: (1) **support three different game *families* in one package** — **Lines & Ways + Jackpot Rush / JPRE-Georgia + Fiery Ice Link Georgia** (Efim's foundational capability, never done before in a single product; Efim floated "GPRG-Georgia" to disambiguate the Jackpot Rush variant from the standard JPRE product — naming not yet canonical); (2) **three unrelated ("non-symbiotic") progressives coexisting in the same package**; (3) the **new match-3 progressive** replacing the legacy Georgia peach progressive bonus on legacy peach-style line/ways games. The latest **any-bit version of Jackpot Rush** is required. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_capability_decomposition-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_capability_decomposition-May-8-2026.md]]
- **Liquid-glass interface** stays under its own pre-existing capability (not one of the three); progressive-controller and data-centre pieces are features, not capabilities. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_capability_decomposition-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_capability_decomposition-May-8-2026.md]]
- **Personas applied to GA-COAM v2 requirements:** operator/master, player, and the new **JVL Product Manager**. First PM requirement — **progressive settings cannot be changed on the fly**; and all three progressives display only in **attract mode**, never simultaneously during play. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_requirements_workflow_and_personas-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_requirements_workflow_and_personas-May-8-2026.md]]
- **Immediate work chain.** Juan meets [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]] to design the match-3 game flow and finishes the requirements/personas (all May 8); [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor]] then drafts framework features (unblocked ~Mon May 11, [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] back in office) and owes Val a **schedule** for the **Thu May 14 9:30am ET** review. Design specs (screen/palette/line-width) already exist with [[wiki/people/dmitriy-khandov.md|Dmitriy Khandov]] and [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]] and need converting into capability/requirement format. §15.8: capability/requirement/product economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_requirements_workflow_and_personas-May-8-2026.md]]

## May 8 2026 — rollout status check: V43 field-trial terms locked, package "engraved in stone", Texas trip slips to May 18

A two-segment sales/commercial status check ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-08-ga-coam-v2-rollout-status-check.md|GA-COAM V2 Rollout Status Check]]; 3:44pm + 4:01pm ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]]). This refines the [[#may-4-2026--july-trial-rollout-plan-10-free-v43-machines-into-top-locations-ahead-of-the-aug-15-show|May-4 trial plan]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_1-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_1-May-8-2026.md]]

- **Product + package (fixed).** 8-game V43 vertical; headline feature = **three non-symbiotic progressive mechanics in one machine**. Package = **4 Lines & Ways + 2 Jackpot Rush + 2 Fiery Ice** — "engraved in stone … untouchable"; Val rejected Felipe's proposed re-mix ("we are operating beyond a tight schedule and a lot of people are nervous and unhappy"). Two additional **premium titles held in reserve** for the Aug 18 show, added only if the June rollout is merely average — Val does not want to burn premium titles if June performs well. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_1-May-8-2026.md]]
- **Placement terms.** ~10 machines (fluid **5–15**) into **10 primary + 5 backup** operators (50–250 locations each), each at the operator's single **top-performing location only** (non-negotiable), **90-day test**, operator keeps all revenue. In exchange JVL requires **QR scans + Col-MID** (the lottery-assigned machine number, name supplied by Felipe) **+ monthly lottery performance reports** (Felipe made the reports non-optional — "otherwise we're going blind") + permission to publicise results. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_1-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_1-May-8-2026.md]]
- **Timeline + marketing.** Target delivery ~**June 7**; the week of **May 18** the team should know if that is realistic, at which point Felipe opens ~2 weeks of concrete operator-by-operator negotiations to close 10–15 placements; **end of June** production rollout; **Aug 18** show with ~6+ weeks of field data as a testimonial. Pre-show anticipation via the **Gamoa newsletter**, message led by the three non-symbiotic progressive mechanics — [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] to draft the marketing wording (using the transcript + Claude). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_1-May-8-2026.md]]
- **Texas Maple (Segment 2).** Felipe's Texas visit slips to the **week of May 18** (May-11 week full — an "Andrei" visiting, likely [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] on transactional-equipment work (inference), plus the Gamala meeting); objective = 3 locations across 3 different masters, fallback contacts Rick and Rich; transactional-equipment testing there deferred; Felipe travels alone. Val's **Flexport-into-Maple-R / QR-code-reader** follow-up (from the May 4/5 meeting) drew blanks from Juan and Felipe, so Val pulled it to route to [[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth]] and [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] separately. §15.8: market/machine/operator/booth economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_2-May-8-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ga_coam_v2_rollout_status_check-Segment_2-May-8-2026.md]]
