## May 15 2026 — Flex-Recycler feature-schedule workflow working session

A ~50-minute Flex-Recycler requirements/scheduling session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-15-flex-recycler-feature-schedule-workflow.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander "Sasha" Biratko]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]), the follow-up to the [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-05-flex-pos-recycler-web-interface-planning.md|May-5 web-interface call]] and the [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-08-ga-coam-v2-requirements-workflow-personas.md|May-8 capability/requirement workflow]]. Product-side substance:

- **Capability maturity.** Andrei reported the player-facing Flex-Recycler capabilities are nearly complete — only **3 minor edge-case capabilities remain**; the rest have full requirements and use cases, player flow covered first. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]]
- **PIN flow decision — player-input, not auto-generated.** Andrei had designed the FlexPay cash-out PIN to be auto-generated and displayed (to minimise friction); Juan pushed back (a player handed a PIN may forget it before returning to cash out), and the flow was changed to **the player enters a PIN and confirms it**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]]
- **Capabilities must be groomed with Juan.** Val's concern: Andrei may be writing capabilities from an *imagined* rather than a *known* understanding of the subject, so every capability must be run past Juan (who knows the domain) before it is treated as final. Andrei + Juan booked a joint grooming session for **Monday May 18** (Juan is out Tuesday). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]]
- **Next milestone = a feature-level schedule for the Flex-Recycler**, reviewed at a checkpoint Val set for **Wednesday May 20 2026, 8:00am ET** (invite to follow). Scheduling of the already-known features ([[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Sasha]] + [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] + Irina) runs **in parallel** with Andrei's remaining capability/requirement work, not after it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]]
- **Gamoa gift-card-to-ATM path on hold.** In pre-meeting clearing, Andrei noted the initiative to let gift cards be used at ATMs (a step toward real cash-out) is on hold pending US mid-term elections; Val deferred it to a separate call. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_feature_schedule_workflow-May-15-2026.md]]

The durable Jira/feature-workflow and scheduling rules from this session (feature/story sizing, feature branches + Saturday merges, Backlog→To-Do semantics, components-not-labels, capability close-out criteria, simple-language rule) are recorded on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (May-15). §15.8: feature/schedule/product-workflow economics only; no named-person compensation.

## May 22 2026 — 3-Progressives schedule rejig: match-3 forked to an experimental branch to hit July 1

A ~47-minute tactical scheduling session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-22-3-progressives-gacoam-schedule-rejig.md|3-Progressives for GA-COAM — Schedule Rejig]], 2:31pm ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor Metelski]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena Zhukova]], [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Anton Leshkevich]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/evgeny-kharetski.md|Evgeny Kharetski]]) walking the active Jira task list for the [[#may-8-2026--ga-coam-v2-capability-decomposition--requirements-workflow-two-alignment-sessions|GA-COAM v2 3-progressive]] release against the **July 1 ship target**. The problem: under the current sequencing [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor]]'s task chain finishes **~June 10** (a Friday), leaving only a weekend to test and hand off — which [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina]] called unrealistic. The team restructured into a parallel-track plan. (Russian transcript, garbled; team worked from the live Jira board — feature numbers reproduced where audio + Pavel's references caught them.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]

- **The match-3 dependency that blocks parallelisation.** The **match-3 progressive bonus** is tightly tied to a specific **progressive controller + data-centre configuration** at engine-initialisation time — the bonus games' math engine needs progressive parameters pre-populated during init, flowing through the data centre. The current **branch v0.24** (where the multi-product work lives) does not know about the new match-3 mechanism, so developing the bonus in v0.24 is blocked behind the long multi-product path. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Solution — a separate experimental branch (parallel-branch decoupling).** Fork an experimental branch from the **GK base (v0.119, the standard GCAM branch** per Irina); add match-3 progressive support to the **Data Centre** and **Progressive Controller** in that branch; then [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor]] + [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Anton]] develop the match-3 bonus **in parallel** with the v0.24 multi-product work, merging later. [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor]] resisted [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]]'s "add fake match-3 support" shortcut — "better to add proper progressive support." In Jira terms: the match-3 chain (**FRA-1195 / FRA-1201 / FRA-1196**) is disconnected from v0.24 and put on the experimental branch. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Stale-feature deletion rule (Val).** [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] flagged **FRA-1005** ("framework master test"), assigned to [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Anton]] long ago and never followed up. Val's rule: *"if it's been sitting there and nobody's asking, it doesn't exist"* — moved to backlog, Anton may re-add if it turns out needed. Val reinforced the broader principle: stale work items get killed, not preserved on hope. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Evgeny's Windows-Docker blocker.** [[wiki/people/evgeny-kharetski.md|Evgeny]] is getting the data centre + framework to launch under Windows Docker locally (so Anton can develop without the physical machine). **RIS** (init flow) now passes — he loaded the 3 real games Igor provided, replacing the 3 dummy stubs — **but normal setup fails post-RIS** (the framework restarts after RIS and the restart crashes). He estimated ~2 more days (ready ~Tue May 26). Crucially, **Anton can start the game-select screen NOW** on the existing single-product launch path (a different branch, not v0.24) without waiting for the multi-product launcher. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Home screen deferred.** The home screen displays multiple games grouped by progressive type, so it requires the multi-product launch path working — it waits until that stabilises. [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena]] confirmed workable (not final) game-select, match-3, and home-screen design assets are all ready **Monday May 25** — she deliberately engineered the asset pipeline so all three workstreams have inputs Monday. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Revised by-person plan.** Anton (week of May 25): game-select screen in single-product mode (FRA-1182 area); from ~June 1: match-3 bonus FRA-1196 in the experimental branch. Igor (week of May 25): continue multi-product + coordinate with [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima Chudinov]] on adding match-3 progressive support to the experimental branch; from ~June 1: return to multi-product / 3-progressive coexistence (FRA-1204 / FRA-1205). Dima Chudinov: add match-3 progressive support to the MBD progressive controller (also finishing Miranda server-side presets). Evgeny: finish the Windows-Docker fix by Tue May 26. Lena: final asset substitution week June 1–5. **Goal: a reviewable build by June 5, final review cycle from June 8, July 1 ship.** Next checkpoint: **Tuesday May 26, 10 AM ET** (Toronto-friendly time), Pavel to send the invite. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_3_progressives_ga_coam_schedule_rejig-May-22-2026.md]]

§15.8: schedule/branch/product engineering economics only; no named-person compensation.

## May 29 2026 — GCOAM Match3 progressive sync: nobody has seen the progressive draw; "Linux-first" to unblock

A ~25-minute status sync ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-29-gcoam-match3-progressive-sync.md|GCOAM Match3 Progressive Sync]], 10:03am ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor Metelski]], [[wiki/people/evgeny-kharetski.md|Evgeny Kharetski]], [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Anton Liashkevich]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena Zhukova]]) on the [[#may-22-2026--3-progressives-schedule-rejig-match-3-forked-to-an-experimental-branch-to-hit-july-1|GA-COAM Match-3 progressive]] game. The headline problem: the team still cannot run and look at the build, so nobody — not Juan, not Michael, not Lena — has seen the progressive working. Everything else flows from that. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GCOAM_Match3_Progressive_Sync-May-29-2026.md]]

- **Why it's blocked.** Anton finished updating the game resources and home screen and is recompiling the games. To view games you need a **RIS** (the config the data centre uses to bring games up), but the current RIS only covers Igor's 3 games, and the **Linux build kept failing** because game files were missing from it, so the machine never came up. During the meeting Anton found the compilation error and committed a fix, and Irina started a fresh Linux build. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GCOAM_Match3_Progressive_Sync-May-29-2026.md]]
- **The agreed plan — Linux first.** Get the build to compile, bring the machine up on **Linux**, and confirm the progressive actually contributes and draws — before doing anything for Windows or designers. (Proposed by Irina, agreed by Lena and the room.) Once Linux works, Evgeny copies the known-good settings for Windows testing and **freezes a Docker image / RIS snapshot** to hand to designers. **Building proper auto-deploy (CI/CD) infrastructure was ruled out for now** — it can't be ready by the June 7 target. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GCOAM_Match3_Progressive_Sync-May-29-2026.md]]
- **Progressive draw is the real unknown.** Evgeny can run the progressive in Docker but has **never successfully drawn (won) a progressive**. He suspects a settings problem but can't prove it until the machine is up. On the framework side Igor reports a **BaseValue that the math recalculates and that doesn't match the progressive's value**, and his frontend throws stack overflows. Igor won't sign off on multiprogressive support until he sees both the data centre and the framework behave correctly end to end. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GCOAM_Match3_Progressive_Sync-May-29-2026.md]]
- **Match3 framework snag — parameter passing at math init.** Igor added baseline framework support for a Match3 progressive, but the **math engine initialises much earlier than the progressive manager**, so progressive parameters (level / centre type, plus a minimum bet) must be passed in at math init — needing an extra request to the data centre. Evgeny offered a quick temp-storage workaround; **Pavel rejected it and insisted on a proper standard API, "no crutches."** (Decision: parameter passing uses a standard API, not a temp-storage shortcut.) [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GCOAM_Match3_Progressive_Sync-May-29-2026.md]]
- **Designer hand-off.** Pavel worried about running the progressive controller on Windows; Evgeny pushed back — it's just Docker, and designers only need teaching to use it (they had Docker Desktop for Cline and can reinstall it). Once the build is testable, Evgeny will write a short step-by-step Docker guide. Pavel called this the smaller problem versus building the Windows games. JIRA references (numbers garbled): ~1195 (Match3 progressive completion), ~1198 (multiprogressive support). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GCOAM_Match3_Progressive_Sync-May-29-2026.md]]

§15.8: build/schedule/product engineering economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Jun 11 2026 — the passport-scan "dsKey not registered" field incident: settle it with data, not argument

A short field-incident call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-06-11-passport-scan-dskey-malfunction.md|Passport Scan / dsKey Malfunction]], ~10:49am ET, ~11 min; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dzmitry Chudzinau]] on the Minsk side; the Georgia field parties [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]] and [[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth]] referenced but not on the call). Russian. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]

- **The symptom and the dispute.** After a software upgrade, scanning a machine's passport returns **"dsKey not registered"** and blocks the machine. Val read his framing back to confirm it: Minsk believes the machine's **Dallas chip was replaced**, so the block is correct; Felipe shows the Dallas was **never replaced**, so the upgrade is misbehaving. The team agreed that is the correct framing. Val said he trusts Felipe's judgment and doubts a Dallas was reused (everyone knows reuse is not allowed and won't work), and suspects the fault is in the software update. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]
- **Dzmitry's counter — reuse does happen in the field.** Dzmitry pushed back that reusing old passports and Dallas chips does happen in the field, fairly often, though the reason is unknown; he pointed to an older thread Sergey had raised about passport/database help that was never gotten to the bottom of (it "somehow started working" and no conclusion was drawn), and admitted he has very little data and doesn't know which chips are involved. Val stayed skeptical that reuse is the cause. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]
- **Resolution — settle it with data (decided).** Pavel's method: read the actual Dallas number from the machine's logs and compare it against what the system's generated registration record says; if they don't match, the problem is on the software side. Ownership: **Val** writes and posts the English directional message in the group Teams chat and sets the compare-both-sides plan; **Minsk (Pavel)** decodes the Dallas number encoded in the generated registration record; **the field (Felipe / Elizabeth)** collects logs from machines **229918 and 230217** to read each machine's actual Dallas number now. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]
- **Val's coaching + framing.** Val gave Dzmitry direct feedback that his answers are too clipped — no direction, no guidance — leaving field people like "deer in the headlights"; plain written English, an acknowledgment, and a clear next step are expected. Val closed on the point that this is not about who is right — machines down cost money, and the goal is to find the root cause and close it for good. The device-registration mechanism (the Dallas number is encoded in the generated registration record and can be decoded and compared to the chip in the field) is on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Jun-11). §15.8: field-incident / device-registration engineering only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]

## Jun 22 2026 — ARC deal debrief: 30-day V43 trial, parallel D27 upgrades, printers-on-purchase-only

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-22-arc-deal-post-meeting-debrief.md|Jun-22 5:15pm ET ARC post-meeting debrief]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]]) Felipe reported back on his meeting with **ARC**, a Georgia operator/prospect. ARC was receptive but wants **proof of performance before committing** to a full deal (Val framed it as a quarter-million-dollar commitment that warrants a trial). ARC is recorded in prose and **queued as a candidate organization** (single-meeting mention — no org page forced). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ARC_Deal_Post_Meeting_Debrief-June-22-2026.md]]

- **The V43 trial.** ARC proposed a **30-day trial of 5 brand-new V43 machines** running the new game. Felipe negotiated placement at quality locations — **at least 2 tier-1, 2 tier-2, and 1 tier-3**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ARC_Deal_Post_Meeting_Debrief-June-22-2026.md]]
- **Parallel D27 upgrade of existing field locations.** Alongside the V43 trial, ARC agreed to upgrade **5 existing in-field locations to the latest D27 software** (fixed watchdog timer + Linux) to demonstrate improved performance of the current product line; **Felipe to work with Tony** to select which locations. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ARC_Deal_Post_Meeting_Debrief-June-22-2026.md]]
- **Printers only on a purchase commitment.** ARC wants **printers (Nanoptics)** included in any purchase deal. Val ruled out installing printers on the 5 trial machines — no point if they are returned after 30 days — so JVL will **promise printers upon purchase commitment**, not during the trial. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ARC_Deal_Post_Meeting_Debrief-June-22-2026.md]]
- **Data-collection / Georgia-lottery concern resolved.** ARC's only concern was whether the **Georgia lottery** would permit machine data collection to an external system. **Juan confirmed** every kiosk currently connected in Georgia already collects data and the lottery has not objected. No modems are available for the trial V43s, but ARC is fine with **QR-code scanning for reporting** and offered full access to its own system reports; George at ARC offered to accompany Felipe to locations personally. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ARC_Deal_Post_Meeting_Debrief-June-22-2026.md]]
- **Priority ordering: the broad V43 rollout beats ARC.** Val was explicit that installing **10 V43s across at least 8 operators by the first week of July** is a higher priority than the ARC deal ("no comparison" on scale); several operators have already committed (some brought their own locks), and with ARC's 5 trial units the total could reach **13–15 machines**. Val also directed Felipe to **install the new V43 software in the shop and personally test it for a couple of days** before any field deployment; **Eclipse approval is expected next week**. Juan noted that by the time ARC's 30-day trial ends, the new games (**Twin Bison, new Lions Ridge**) will be ready in August and ARC will get those updates free. §15.8 clear — operator/deal/machine economics only; no named-person compensation recorded. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_ARC_Deal_Post_Meeting_Debrief-June-22-2026.md]]

## Jul 7 2026 — Georgia market intel (competitor "Access" cards, operator sentiment) and the inventory/pipeline review

On a brief [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-07-ga-coam-inventory-pipeline-review.md|Jul-7 5:03pm ET review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]]) Felipe relayed what competitors are selling in Georgia and reviewed the current pipeline. The competitor payout-device angle (the "Access" loyalty card, Payline/AppShots' pulse method) is also on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Jul-7). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Inventory_Pipeline_Review-July-7-2026.md]]

- **What competitors are selling — "Access."** The buzz in Georgia is all about **"Access" solutions**: a **loyalty/players card that converts to a gift card**, run off a small monitor mounted on the machine (like a Kroger POS). Pricing is about **$100/month per location of 6 machines** — roughly **$15/machine/month** — with the equipment nearly free. It used to cost more when **Tyson** was the only distributor selling Access to masters in Georgia; now there are **three main sellers — Genesis, Raul, and Tyson**, with **Raul the best-known and cheapest** and selling a lot. Felipe will call Raul to get exact installation and device pricing. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Inventory_Pipeline_Review-July-7-2026.md]]
- **The "income" solution is a fallback.** Some operators took the "income" solution to meet the deadline, but it is seen as a fallback when nothing better is available — not much demand. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Inventory_Pipeline_Review-July-7-2026.md]]
- **Operator sentiment — moving off kiosks.** Felipe spoke to **Salim** (formerly VP at Gomoa, now with **Dixie**): operators are unhappy with current solutions, especially **kiosks**, and everyone is moving toward something simpler. **Payline / AppShots** is gaining ground because it needs only one printer off the Mother Goose and sends credits by pulse — **similar to JVL's approach** — though many still prefer Access for its transaction security and its ability to connect both SaaS and non-SaaS machines. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Inventory_Pipeline_Review-July-7-2026.md]]
- **Epic trial — a second machine, and why the QR code matters.** Epic picked up a **second machine** for the trial, likely installed the next day. Owner **Kenny** is hard to reach, but his right-hand man **Dustin** approved it and will provide the **call ID and QR code**. The QR code is load-bearing — **without it JVL doesn't get paid per game** — so Felipe made a tutorial telling operators to scan the code and also send a backup photo (the photo carries the serial number needed to connect). Felipe is also still trying to reach **Ryan Deaton**, who has been dodging calls. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Inventory_Pipeline_Review-July-7-2026.md]]
- **Pipeline / forecast — Georgia only (decision).** June actuals in Georgia: **9 machines**. July forecast: **15 to 20 games, of which 15 are sold and going on test** — separate from the **14 V43 units**, roughly **29 games in total**. Val is tracking **Georgia only** for this review and is **not counting** the 10 South Dakota (MG Oil) machines or the 1 in Texas that Felipe also cited. §15.8 clear — market/competitor/operator and machine economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Inventory_Pipeline_Review-July-7-2026.md]]

## Jul 15 2026 — version-4119 touchscreens stop working, blocking the ARC roll-out (morning triage → afternoon investigation)

Two same-day sessions worked a **field defect blocking the roll-out to the Georgia operator [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|ARC]]** — touchscreens stop working after machines are upgraded to **software version 4119**. [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]] (transcribed "Philippe"), who installs the software in the field, had emailed a list of the affected machines and said he had flagged it before. The two attendees on both calls were the Minsk engineers [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] and [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]] with [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]. The morning is the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-15-version-4119-touchscreen-loss-triage.md|11:01am triage]]; the afternoon is the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-15-gacoam-touchscreen-investigation-check-back.md|3:01pm check-back]] (two distinct sessions on the same defect, not re-transcriptions).

- **Firmware-version theory ruled out (morning).** The first theory was that the touchscreens' firmware version was wrong — a similar problem years earlier had been fixed by reflashing problem touchscreens from a laptop. The firmware version of each machine is recorded in its "passport"; the affected machines' firmware **matched other, working machines**, so the theory was set aside. Reproduction was attempted on three machines and **failed**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Version_4119_Touchscreen_Loss_Triage-July-15-2026.md]]
- **Leading theory becomes the USB port / mapping (morning) — then dropped (afternoon).** Pavel's theory was that some touchscreens are plugged into a different USB port and the new software changed how ports are detected, breaking the touchscreen-to-display mapping. Val pushed the team to reason through the software mapping rather than keep physically reproducing on machines. By the afternoon Pavel had an AI compare a machine on the old version against one on the new version; the mapping came back **identical**, so the USB-mapping theory was **dropped**. A newly-found difference — each touchscreen gaining a **duplicate mouse-emulation device** on the new version — was also **ruled out** as a cause. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Version_4119_Touchscreen_Loss_Triage-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GACOAM_Touchscreen_Investigation_Check_Back-July-15-2026.md]]
- **Leading suspect now: the boards being re-flashed during install (afternoon).** A separate install failure — a **"peripheral device fail"** message — appears when the **iBoard or counter-board** is not found; both are mandatory for the install to continue. During install these boards (iBoard, LED controller, counter-board) are also **re-flashed**, which takes time, and the machine may progress past the point where it still reads them. The agreed experiment: flash **old** firmware onto at least 3 boards, install an old software version (e.g. 17.76/17.70 or a 19.x), then install the new version on top, to test whether the background board-flashing during install is what causes the intermittent failures. Pavel estimated ~1–1.5 days. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GACOAM_Touchscreen_Investigation_Check_Back-July-15-2026.md]]
- **The installer hides the background flashing.** Val objected that the installer gives no on-screen feedback about the board flashing/validation (only disk checksums are shown); extended logs exist but require plugging in a keyboard. He called it a weak decision but ruled **not to redesign the installer now** — the priority is finding the bug faster. Field data was to come from Felipe/"Philippe", who would boot a faulting machine with **two USB sticks** and capture the logs (logs only run once the machine reaches the init stage, not during install mode). Irina was to send Pavel an AI write-up of the differences between the two software ISOs for his review. §15.8 clear — field-defect / device / roll-out engineering only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GACOAM_Touchscreen_Investigation_Check_Back-July-15-2026.md]]

## Jul 17 2026 — the Aug-1 Mutha-Goose launch design: 5 IQ devices at 5 locations, and keeping a location running if IQ dies

On a [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-17-iq-down-printer-bypass.md|Jul-17 9:00 AM ET session]] (~2h13m, Russian; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], with [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] joining ~23 min in) the team locked the field design for the first on-location Flex-IQ launch. The device/decision detail is on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Jul-17). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]

- **The launch — 5 devices at 5 locations on August 1.** JVL launches Flex-IQ on **Mutha Goose locations on August 1, 2026 — 5 devices at 5 locations**, about two weeks out from this meeting. At each, the IQ unit sits in-line between the Mutha Goose game controller and the ticket printer. Val stressed the whole launch design is **only** for Aug-1; capability expansions come later once the market is understood. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Keeping a location running if IQ dies (the field fail-safe).** If Flex-IQ fully dies, Mutha Goose beeps within seconds and the cashier connects the printer directly to Mutha Goose (which prints its cached tickets), so the location keeps operating the old way. Val ruled JVL will **not** instruct location staff on that wire swap, and a replacement IQ starts fresh with **no history pulled from Mutha Goose** — so JVL must instead see failures remotely (red/blinking tilt screen, dashboard status). At launch the flow is **card issuance by default; only on card failure can the cashier retry or print a standard Mutha Goose ticket** that returns settlement to the old system. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Georgia payout rules in play.** Since **July 1**, Georgia locations may **not** pay out in merchandise; **lottery tickets and gift cards remain allowed**. The over-$1000 cashout case is unhandled (INCOMM's limit is **$1000 per card**; wins above $1200 are taxable; whether Georgia even has a jackpot limit is unclear) — all delegated to [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] to work with INCOMM. §15.8 clear — Georgia field-deployment / device / market economics only (INCOMM card limits, the $2 activation fee); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]

## Jul 17 2026 — corrupt Intralot COAM report data: legacy pay-table-ID co-mingling, and QR code as the only source of truth

On the weekly [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-17-game-design-stages-georgia-data.md|Jul-17 2:00 PM ET Game_Development meeting]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim Podvoiski]]) Juan walked through visibly corrupt data in the Georgia Intralot COAM lottery report — reinforcing why JVL trusts only its own QR-code numbers. (The game-design half of the same meeting is on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Jul-17).) [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]

- **The report is visibly wrong.** Juan pulled an Intralot report for **July 6–16**, filtered to software release **V43/V2**. Machines installed only 4–8 days ago show hundreds of "active days" (e.g. **419**, 140) and impossible totals — **Rodeo Spins** shows ~**$17 million wagered** on a handful of machines installed 8 days ago, and its games-played count (~**50,000**) can't reconcile with $17M wagered. Val's quip: they could advertise "$17 million wagered in 4 days — if you don't believe us, ask GLC." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Problem 1 — legacy pay-table-ID co-mingling.** Re-released titles keep the **same pay table ID** because their **math never changed** (the ID is a **6-digit alphanumeric** tied to the mathematical structure, not the release). So Intralot appears to be **co-mingling** old (legacy) data with new data for titles that existed before — **Bayou Riches, Crazy Cult, Queen of Luxor** look "dirty" (blown active-day counts), while brand-new titles look clean. Michael's framing: the game was deployed in Georgia ~1.5 years ago, got graphic updates (Liquid Glass, Match the Progressive) in the new release, but the ID didn't change, so the data reads as contiguous. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Problem 2 — a second, unexplained error.** Val and Efim established there is **more than one problem**: even combined legacy data can't produce the $17–70M figures for this pay table over the period, and the wagered totals don't match games-played — so something else is wrong too, possibly Intralot's **filters** (the 10-day/software-family filter may not be working) or **how they pull the data**. JVL doesn't know how Intralot pulls its numbers. Juan noted a misconfigured board (wrong dip switch) can multiply bets and worsen over time — but the report is supposed to rely on **SAS 1**, not the board. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Is SAS 1 really GLC's source of truth?** Juan questioned whether GLC relies **100%** on SAS 1. Val: supposedly yes — GLC mandated SAS 1 (prohibited for others, with extra machine-disabling functions, for GLC's exclusive use), and logically it is the only source of truth — but "in Georgia nothing is 100%," and he isn't certain. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Proposed fix — a unique pay-table ID for V3 re-releases (a theory to test).** Juan suggested assigning a **new/unique pay table ID** (e.g. adding a "B" or a "2" suffix) to re-released themes in **Georgia V3** — the math can stay unchanged — to stop Intralot pulling in old-game data. Michael: starting fresh at least lets you reconcile the data against the games themselves. Framed as an **experiment**, not a decision. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]
- **QR code remains the only source of truth; 12 machines installed.** Val reaffirmed JVL does **not** rely on this Intralot report — only its own **QR-code data**. First QR scans are expected **next week** (~week of Jul 20); Juan will keep updating Georgia performance as scans come in. **12 machines are now installed** in Georgia. Juan doesn't know whether operators/masters see the same corrupt report, or whether the $17M would translate to the financial report the masters see. Juan will also go back to Intralot about the impossible numbers and check pay-table-ID handling with Pavel and Irina. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Design_Stages_Georgia_Data-July-17-2026.md]]

## Jul 24 2026 — Georgia V3 submission target: aim for Aug 17 (the show), but QA gates it

On the weekly [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-24-game-production-schedule-georgia-v3.md|Jul-24 2:00 PM ET Game_Development meeting]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena Zhukova]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim Podvoiski]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]]) the team set the plan for submitting **Georgia V3** to **Eclipse**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Production_Schedule_Georgia_V3-July-24-2026.md]]

- **Target submission ~Monday Aug 17, but only after thorough QA.** Juan's ideal date is **Aug 17** — the **Georgia show runs Tue Aug 18 – Fri Aug 21**, and Eclipse approvals have been averaging **five business days or less**, so an Aug-17 submission could be approved by the show's end. Michael wants more time if needed: **V2 was submitted aggressively, shipped with bugs, and field deployment had to be stopped several times**, so he wants **Ivan to QA V3 thoroughly before submission** — approval-by-show's-end is the goal, not a hard deadline. Juan agreed; **Twin Bison and Lions Riches** are expected to be ready to show at the Georgia show either way. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Game_Production_Schedule_Georgia_V3-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Release roadmap and a coming breather.** Working assumption: **Georgia V3 mid-August, South Dakota to market ~September 1, Nebraska mid-October**. After those, nothing is pressing — the remaining verticals (South Dakota, Nebraska, Texas) are not customer-driven — so the team can slow down and put more time into technical debt; the transactional product should also be released by then. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Game_Production_Schedule_Georgia_V3-July-24-2026.md]]

The V3 game contents, Twin Bison math, Mega Hit Blaze branding, South Dakota / D27 VLT2, and build-tool technical debt from the same meeting are on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Jul-24). **§15.8:** clear — release-schedule / show-timing economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Jul 27 2026 — InComm reseller models for the COAM gift-card market; the master-relationship sensitivity

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-27-incomm-prod-location-registration.md|Jul-27 InComm call]] ([[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] with a full InComm crew) InComm framed how JVL enters the **Georgia COAM gift-card reseller market**. JVL will pursue **both models** — acting as the reseller itself (it has a merchant ID) and selling kiosks into locations that already have a broker agreement — so JVL must integrate to whichever ecosystem a location runs: **RWJ** (highest location count; its own "Yugo Hub"/"PayAnyUp" middleware, not RTG), **Yinlow** (own middleware), or **Lunex** and large self-reseller operators (InComm **RTG**, passing that party's merchant ID). Full InComm/registration detail on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]] (Jul-27). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_InComm_PROD_Location_Registration-July-27-2026.md]]

- **The master-relationship sensitivity.** As a COAM device manufacturer JVL cannot engage a location directly — it goes **through the master**, who owns the location relationship. JVL acting as a **direct gift-card reseller** would create a direct location relationship and could **offend a master who also buys JVL gaming equipment** — Juan flagged this as a delicate topic to manage as JVL rolls the reseller business into the market. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_InComm_PROD_Location_Registration-July-27-2026.md]]

**§15.8:** clear — reseller-model / market-entry economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Jul 30 2026 — production-ramp forecast through Dec 31: ~200 Flex bases, metal covers 100 (Oct-1 the critical date), 43-inch air-freight

On a ~1h28m [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-30-georgia-production-ramp-forecast.md|Jul-30 2:14 PM ET manufacturing review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/mikhail-perelman.md|Mikhail Perelman]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth Palomino]], with [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]] translating in the second half) the team worked the Georgia production ramp for Flex, Echo and countertop machines through **Dec 31**. This continues the [[wiki/topics/echo-home-gaming-device.md|Jul-23/27 Echo-build and manufacturing]] thread. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]

- **The forecast — ~200 Flex bases to year-end.** The working forecast is **~200 Flex bases** through Dec 31. Mikhail sized shortages against 200 units at the major-assembly level: **base unit 200, monitor (D27) assembly 80, the 43-inch unit 150**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **Metal covers ~100 machines → Oct-1 is the critical date.** The biggest risk is metal, but Elizabeth confirmed metal on hand covers **~100 machines**, carrying production through **August, September and into October**. So the sensitive window runs only **to Oct 1**; up to that point there are essentially no metal issues, and any gaps can be covered by air or ocean freight. The full 200 only matters for the Jan-1 horizon. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **43-inch touchscreen is the one clear long-lead item.** Only **15 in stock**, ~**1 month** vendor lead (Mikhail can push the vendor "Christina" toward ~3 weeks), and it must be **air-freighted** for September builds — about **30 pieces would need to fly for September**, which Mikhail accepted as manageable (August needs only ~16). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **Countertops fully covered except Amazon harnesses.** Countertop parts are all in stock except some **standard harnesses from Amazon** — not a concern. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **Assembly capacity is sufficient (detail on the Echo page).** Elizabeth's deliberately conservative capacity model shows enough labor even on pessimistic numbers; Val accepted it "belt and suspenders." Full model on [[wiki/topics/echo-home-gaming-device.md]] (Jul-30). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **Hiring plan — start with six, settle near three strong.** Elizabeth had approval to hire **four** temporary people; Val recommended starting with **six**, expecting to cut the weak ones and end with about **three strong** performers, which with the **two existing** people makes ~**five**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **Sebastian Cortes → Georgia Oct 1 as an engineer, not warehouse.** Sebastian transfers to Georgia on **Oct 1** for service and product-facing work (he can take service calls remotely before then); Val refused to put him on warehouse duty ("like feeding a child with a golden spoon" — his engineering value belongs in front of customers). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]
- **One dedicated kitting/warehouse person + daily floor hygiene.** Genadi recommended one organized (not expert) person to run kitting and warehouse hygiene — bringing parts to the assembly floor and returning leftovers — with **daily** cleanup, not weekly, so the floor isn't flooded with parts. Elizabeth also needs basic tool kits for new hires and lockers for staff; Val believes lockers were already shipped and will check. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Production_Ramp_Forecast-July-30-2026.md]]

Next meeting: Val + Mikhail on **Monday Aug 3** (a Canadian holiday). **§15.8:** clear — unit-forecast / parts / lead-time / build-capacity economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Jul 31 2026 — early Georgia field performance: hold running high, spread smooth, and the Nebraska-next pivot

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-31-georgia-game-math-performance-review.md|Jul-31 2:00 PM ET game-development review]] ([[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim Podvoiski]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena Zhukova]]) the team reviewed the early Georgia field numbers. The game-math tuning behind them is on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Jul-31). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]

- **Data is far too early to conclude (Juan).** Some machines have only **2–6 days** in the field, the longest **16 days**; Juan wants another **~30 days**. He color-codes on ~**$1,500 coin-in** and ~**$200/day** as a solid number. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]
- **Hold is running high in JVL's favor.** Games are set at **92% base + 2% to the meter = 94% all-in**. If field numbers slip, the plan is to **cut the progressive 1% before touching base percentages**, keeping games close to the well-performing original Peach setups. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]
- **Per-title spread.** Spread across games is smooth; **Ocho** swung from bottom to top depending on location; **Rodeo Queen** is lower than expected (Michael's theory: the market is ~70% male and women bet less/less aggressively); **Arc** is a standout at **$4–5k coin-in days**; Jackpot Rush titles with "Add Any Bet" improve as meters run. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]
- **Submission slips a week or two past mid-August.** Given the remaining math/messaging/attract-video/bug work and Anton's vacation, Michael recommended pushing the Georgia submission out **a week or two past mid-August** to get it right; Juan agreed. A flagged risk: Jackpot Rush still shows "max bet required to win" messaging even though it now pays on any bet — incorrect messaging that may be dampening play and must be fixed before submission. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]
- **Economy hardware experiment — "spectacular."** Val reported a substantial experiment with the intended "economy" hardware board: it is not a faster processor but a different **RK-based (Chinese) architecture** — the same used for the transactional products — running **six games at 60 FPS**. The only issue was a momentary freeze on the base-to-bonus transition, traced to minimal memory; they are expanding to **16 GB**. FP7 remains the high-end platform. (Michael noted Stern Pinball similarly migrated to Raspberry Pi.) [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]
- **Nebraska is the next battleground (from September).** After **Georgia V3**, Val wants the focus to shift to **Nebraska starting September** — a fertile market that would immediately help the bottom line. Nebraska is currently not performing and they have eliminated the 25-cent bets; South Dakota is performing "okay" and should improve with Kinos out (PD still owes South Dakota, paused for Georgia). Val was candid that he feels "frozen"/overwhelmed by the number of simultaneous initiatives (hardware switch, Novo/Omni platform move, the new "Galaxy" backend, and changed file-keeping/production-push processes all at once). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Game_Math_Performance_Review-July-31-2026.md]]

**§15.8:** clear — game-math / field-performance / hardware economics only; no named-person compensation.

Target: dispenser + recycler builds done no later than **Wednesday evening (Aug 5)**, Andrei helping Elizabeth and coordinating with Pavel. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Expo_Prep_Alignment-August-3-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Expo_Prep_Alignment-August-3-2026.md]]
- **Customer agreements (Juan).** A two-tier structure basically done: a **master agreement** (bulk of the legal language, governed by Georgia law, service-fee table renewable on 30-day notice, reviewed once by the customer's legal team) plus a light **sales agreement** that references the executed master and just lists hardware/serial numbers, needing no legal review. Hardware pricing sits on the sales agreement, service fees on the master. Detail on [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Expo_Prep_Alignment-August-3-2026.md]]

Duala also won't release the updated service-fee structure until the contract is finalized. The website privacy policy also needs to go to Duala for acceptance. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Expo_Prep_Alignment-August-3-2026.md]]
- **Recycler — MEI concept problem.** MEI's recycler forces the primary (largest) cassette to hold the lowest denomination filled to 100% — all $5s, the least-inserted bill. Val wants the opposite: primary cassette in **$20s** (to give change mostly in 20s), accept 1/5/10/20, route all **$1s straight to a drop box** (not stacked), stack 5/10/20 in the secondary cassette. Juan will call MEI to request the reprogramming; if MEI won't accommodate, JVL can't use their unit. Plan B is the **ICT** unit (needs mechanical fitting, more expensive); **JCM** is barely responsive. **Herring Bank** hardware waits until after the show. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Georgia_Expo_Prep_Alignment-August-3-2026.md]]
- **Show demo flow + fake-bill warning.** For the recycler the flow is e-wallet-to-cash with no real card issued (pay cash → issue a QR → carry it through the machines → cash out to the QR → present at the recycler → dispense), the on-screen steps faked. Val strongly warned Andrei to discard the realistic fake bills he had bought — recounting a former employee criminally charged and convicted for carrying fake bills — and directed the machine dispense **blank pages** sized to fit and deliberately not resembling currency. Juan noted competitors use "funny money" at shows; Val insisted against it. ## Aug 10 2026 — LTE modem on the V-1000 for Georgia COAM: fold into GA-COAM V3, but antenna mounting is the hard part

On [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-10-lte-modem-v1000-georgia-coam.md|LTE modem on V-1000]] (9:34–9:57 AM ET, in Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]) Val asked what is needed to get the LTE cellular modem working on the V-1000 machines for Georgia COAM (and Nebraska). This advances the QR-vs-cellular-modem monitoring thread. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_V-1000_Georgia_COAM-August-10-2026.md]]

- **Software is essentially done.** What remains is to assemble the build for Georgia and Nebraska and QA-test it. On the Linux side they added new kernel modules, scripts to run modem commands, and utilities for two modem types; the datacenter GUI now enables/disables the modem, has a manual mode for modem/network parameters, and connect/disconnect with status; the main setup and diagnostics screens show signal strength, connect state and a ping test. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_V-1000_Georgia_COAM-August-10-2026.md]]
- **Fold it into GA-COAM V3 (decided).** Pavel suggested carrying the modem support inside the **GA-COAM V3** build that has to go to the lab anyway, so the modem is a "free addition" in that submission rather than a separate firmware release. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_V-1000_Georgia_COAM-August-10-2026.md]]
- **Physical installation is the hard part.** The modem is a card that plugs into a mating connector on the V-1000 motherboard (open the CPU box, likely remove the IO board to reach the connector, insert the modem, insert the SIM under the modem). The antenna is ~30 cm and must route through an opening, but the cabinet is all metal and the screen is shielded, so antenna placement is a problem. Options: mount internally toward the front/plastic areas; an external antenna through a rear hole; or a small discreet black plastic box on the rear to hold the modem (keeps the standard 30 cm cable short). Cables can be found longer, but antennas can't be extended. Pavel will raise the mounting approach with Genadi. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_V-1000_Georgia_COAM-August-10-2026.md]]
- **Test on real Georgia hardware (decided).** The software was tested only on the South Dakota build; it must be tested on the specific Georgia (V3) build and on **real Georgia machines with a real modem**, because the same vendor's modem for different markets can be incompatible (if a chip/processor differs they may connect remotely and add support, as done with Flex IQ). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_V-1000_Georgia_COAM-August-10-2026.md]]
- **Timeline.** Val asked for Aug 18; Pavel is not sure V3 ships by then — designer tasks (new resources, per-game demo/attract videos) run through Aug 25, with attract videos ~Aug 17–25. Realistic landing is **end of August**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_V-1000_Georgia_COAM-August-10-2026.md]]

## Aug 11 2026 — the sales pipeline: 14-cabinet second wave, ~50/month scale-up, V3+modem, and the crashing-game fight

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-11-ga-coam-pipeline-trial-forecast.md|Aug-11 1:03pm ET pipeline call]] (~47 min; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]]) Val and Felipe set the trial/sales plan for the coming months. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]

- **August second-wave trial = 14 cabinets across 5 operators (decided).** United Gaming 5, Legends Amusement 3, Continental 2, Mariani Amusement 2, Jaya Game Amusements 2. Val raised Continental from 1 to 2 — his reasoning: one machine failing is a 100% failure, two gives "a leg to stand on," and small operators are likely to buy when given more. Of these, only **Legends and Mariani** are firmly committed; Continental and Jaya want to see the game at the mid-August show first; United is not confirmed (the largest Georgia operator, but decisions sit above Felipe's contacts). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **August sales forecast = 12 cabinets.** 5 to **Diamond Amusement** (trading in 5 Altera cabinets at a $1,500 credit each, strictly one-for-one), 5 to **Arc** (meeting its executives this week; possibly 5 more in September), 1 to **DGKL Amusements** (quote sent), 1 already closed to **Allenson** (the old $5,000 credit; payment expected this/next week). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Val rejected loose show estimates.** Felipe's guesses of ~20 cabinets from show conversations in September and 15–20 in October were dismissed as "not built on anything." Val's method instead: work operator by operator — how many machines each has, the strength of the relationship, and what deal converts each ("**point of tilt**" — every operator "will eventually bend if you give them a good enough deal"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Scale trials to ~50 machines/month from September (decided).** Framed as waves: wave 1 done, wave 2 = the current 14 (on current software), wave 3 ≈ 50 machines in September. Math: from Felipe's ~80-operator list (90–95% with payment history), narrow to a reliable list of ~60–75, then run ~25 operators × 2 machines ≈ 50/month for September, October and November — none in December. Val's next review is the first 25, then the next 25, then the next 25. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Show/September product = V3 + modem (decided).** V3 = the same games Felipe has now plus two premium games — **Twin Bison** ("fiery link") and **Lions Riches** — bundled with the modem, shown at the mid-August show. Approval target ~**September 1** (Eclipse historically approves in ~3 days + a letter ~2 days later). The **modem replaces the QR/barcode** data-collection method: operators sign a data-collection waiver and in exchange get the price-list discount. Val stressed that written discount is a **framework/guideline** for structuring deals (the written version was specific to Arc), not a fixed per-customer rate — VIP discount and trade-in terms apply only where they fit. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **The crashing game — response and a heated exchange.** The current 14 cabinets show a crash tied to **one game in the new family**; the team reproduced it (Val: a positive sign — this is the [[wiki/topics/gacoam-game-crash-bug.md|"Tilt-1" crash]] Irina/Pavel reproduced earlier the same day). Priorities: immediately disable the problematic game if it can be isolated, fix the bug, push the fix to fielded machines, and ship the second wave with known bugs fixed; removing the game is a last resort. Felipe questioned rushing V1 out with a known crash; Val pushed back hard — he would never knowingly ship crashing games, he is **"burning $1 million a month"** and "fighting for survival," so machines must move now (upgrades come free once the fix is ready). Val took a five-minute pause to cool down. *(The "$1M a month" is Val's heated, rounded figure to Felipe; the sober cash-basis forecast on [[wiki/topics/jvl-2026-funding-crisis-capital-call.md]] puts net burn far lower — ~$2.5M over ~17 months to Dec-2027 — recorded as different framings, not a contradiction.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **The selling approach Val coached.** Position the trial in the operator's interest: while JVL is still flexible about free trials, lock the deal in now, start putting "30 days of free money" in the operator's pocket, and upgrade the software automatically when the new version ships — the operator loses nothing, JVL captures the momentum. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Production against a 50/month target.** Felipe reported production is improving: temporary help handles pre-assembly, work is organized into teams (Anibal on Echo, Andres training others, Adam on repairs), and pre-assembly for Echo and Videx is moving well, so final assembly is much simpler. Val's earlier worry about whether Elizabeth can produce 50/month is eased. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_COAM_Pipeline_Trial_Forecast-August-11-2026.md]]

## Aug 11 2026 — V3 release schedule: Aug-18 target, attraction-mode videos, drop the Queen of Luxor refresh

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-11-v3-ga-coam-release-schedule.md|Aug-11 5:00pm ET V3 schedule review]] (~21 min; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/michael-gottlieb.md|Michael Gottlieb]], [[wiki/people/lena.md|Lena Zhukova]]) Val reviewed the V3 GA-COAM release ahead of the trade show. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]]

- **Release target Tuesday Aug 18; show setup Aug 18, opens Aug 19 (decided, excluding Queen of Luxor).** V3 ships with **15 games available, 10 shown on screen at once**, running on an **LTE modem**. Judged realistic once the Queen of Luxor refresh is excluded. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Attraction (idle) mode is the main open work.** A full-screen demo playing a short recorded clip of each game; the team is recording advertising/gameplay video for all 15 games (Felipe's request). In the loop **MegaHit** plays first, then each game's logo followed by a full-screen gameplay clip; Val had the order reversed so it now starts with **Safari Iceland** and **Jackpot**. **Vitaliy and Natalia** do the recording (~7 videos each, ~2 days); Lena expects all videos by Friday Aug 14. **Igor** inserts the videos — ~1 day of work, since the template is built and the build uses placeholder dummy video, so it is mostly a file swap. Lena stretched integration to Monday as a buffer; Val insisted the team work the weekend. *(Which Igor is not stated; by build/frontend context most likely [[wiki/people/igor-metelski.md|Igor Metelski]] — not confirmed, flagged.)* [[raw/Meeting_Notes_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Drop the Queen of Luxor graphical refresh for the show (decided).** The game has an old look and a new graphical refresh was prepared but isn't ready; Lena had already agreed that morning with Irina and Pavel not to hand the new assets to programmers, keeping the existing stable version. Val endorsed it — stability first. The new assets will be finished, with a later call on whether they go into V3 or the next release. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]]
- **QA-vs-art disagreement (unresolved).** Val relayed that testers say they cannot start because the art keeps changing; Lena and Michael pushed back that changes at this stage are minimal, the game logic is done, and QA can test the pays now (art tweaks might cause a graphical glitch but not affect logic). Val acknowledged testers still burn setup hours each time but did not want to dwell on it. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]]
- **Demo ISO for Felipe hoped for Monday Aug 17.** Val questioned handing over an untested ISO; Juan said it is only for demonstration and QA may still find errors. The team is still tracking down a **crash bug** (same thread as above) and expected more information the next morning, working overnight. **Jackpot Rush** is doing well — 10–15 progressive hits — going from a game Felipe didn't love to a top game. Val's overall direction: **introduce nothing new** from here to the show; keep the machines stable. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_V-3_GA-COAM_Release_Schedule-August-11-2026.md]]

## Aug 13 2026 — show booth setup, and the Flex-port failure Val hadn't been told about

On a ~13-minute booth-prep call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-08-13-show-booth-setup-port-status.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]]; the invited "Top 25 operators" review was parked) Felipe walked the layout for the Georgia show (product aimed at the Nebraska and Texas markets). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]]

- **Maple has no Flex port; Flex is the demo unit.** The Maple dispenser has no faceplate/bracket for a Flex port (never designed). Val's call: do **not** put a physical port in Maple — show it as-is, explain the port installs when a buyer chooses Flex, and do all live showings on Flex. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]]
- **Pyramid printer broadens the offer.** Felipe worried a pyramid printer in a machine might undercut the push for transactional (Flex-port) products; Val disagreed — it broadens the offer, no wrong image. Val wants **Flex and Maple each shown with a pyramid printer (at least one each), plus a Flex port in one Flex unit** for Nebraska and/or Texas. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]]
- **Layout and count.** ~14 machines: 10–12 V43 (showing the new V43 game), 2 D27, 2 Maple, plus one recycler, one pay/dispenser, one IQ. Four V43s sit center, dressed with plexiglass and lights to showcase the games; each corner gets one transactional device with two ported machines to move around; all machines get toppers. All carry a Flex port except the 2 Maples and the 4 center V43s (left unconnected). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]]
- **Show software fallback.** The show software isn't confirmed ready (expected Monday, possibly Tuesday morning). Felipe will preload the current V2 trial software ("**Mega Hits Blaze**") on all machines as a fallback and load **V43/V3** if ready in time. Setup: round hanging banner assembled Monday noon; move-in Tuesday 8–9 AM. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]]
- **The Flex ports are not working — Val was out of the loop.** Felipe mentioned in passing that the **Flex ports are not working and are being shipped back to Canada for the developer to fix** (they couldn't be fixed remotely). Val had **not heard this** and pushed back hard on why it was never put to him in writing or on screen; Felipe said he is not the owner of it — [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei]] is — and Val moved to reach Andrei. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Show_Booth_Setup_and_Port_Status-August-13-2026.md]]

**Trade-show networking (Aug 13).** Separately, Val cold-called an industry veteran, "**Jim**" (owner of a small company; in the industry since 1982; company 33 years old), to set up a dinner during the show week — settled on Monday night, both with wives ([[wiki/meetings/2026-08-13-jim-dinner-georgia-trade-show.md]]). No business discussed; **Jim's surname and company are unknown and he is queued unresolved** for the owner (§4). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Jim_Dinner_Georgia_Trade_Show-August-13-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Jim_Dinner_Georgia_Trade_Show-August-13-2026.md]]

## Aug 21 2026 — post-show read: the recycler-as-ATM pitch lands, "within three years of a smashing success"

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-21-multiplayer-cabinet-concepts-nova-strategy.md|Aug-21 strategy call]] (mostly a cabinet-concepts discussion — see [[wiki/topics/multiplayer-cabinet-concept.md]]) Val gave the game team his Georgia read from the trade show. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Multiplayer_Cabinet_Concepts_NOVA_Strategy-August-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Multiplayer_Cabinet_Concepts_NOVA_Strategy-August-21-2026.md]]

- **The recycler reframed as a "redemption center with built-in ATM."** Val's pitch to operators, small to large: your **bill acceptors and printers today are a cost center**; the recycler / ATM makes them a **profit center** via **$3–$5 per-transaction ATM fees** plus **recurring monthly revenue (~$65–$100/month, "nobody even flinched")**. He said operators "are in awe" and treat it as a **killer application** for Georgia. When he pitched **Arc**'s COO it took ~3.5 minutes before the COO paused and pulled in the company's **CEO and CFO** mid-pitch. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Multiplayer_Cabinet_Concepts_NOVA_Strategy-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Market read.** Georgia operators are keen to move **away from TITO and bill acceptors** toward a **wallet / e-wallet** solution (running remote 5.9-machine locations on cash is expensive); Val would keep the peripheral dashboard for the first introduction and **let the market pull JVL toward e-wallet** rather than forcing it. He sees the combination of **Flex IQ + Flexport + SMIBs (possibly given away free) + recycler/ATM + the future back-to-back slant-top** positioning JVL for "substantial business" in what he framed as a **~45,000-machine market**, putting JVL **"within three years of a smashing success."** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Multiplayer_Cabinet_Concepts_NOVA_Strategy-August-21-2026.md]]
- **V3 vs NOVA for Georgia.** Georgia's V3 stays on the current platform (Sept 1 target — *execution update, owner 2026-09-05: the **Eclipse** lab submission slipped to **September 15 2026**; the Sept-1 plan of record is unchanged, the revised actual is recorded beside it*); NOVA would be a later, "V4-like" Georgia release (per the [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-14-first-nova-product-south-dakota-vertical.md|Aug-14 sequencing]]). The Georgia **trial "looks solid despite three bugs found,"** and Val wants **LTE modems in every machine** for service/performance/player intelligence. The team is awaiting **Felipe and Andrei's** report on the Georgia V3 software from the show. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Multiplayer_Cabinet_Concepts_NOVA_Strategy-August-21-2026.md]]

## Aug 21 2026 — post-GAMOA machine-sales strategy: the show log, ARC and Bulldog, ILERS footprint-only, and a September forecast Val would not take to the bank

The second recording of the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-21-post-goam-show-recap.md|Aug-21 post-GAMOA recap]] (5:45–6:41 PM ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]]) turned into a hard exchange about whether there is a real plan to sell machines in September, October and November. The rulings are [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-21-convert-vertical-machines-instead-of-new-trials.md]] and [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-21-cell-modem-on-every-machine-from-september-1.md]].

**Felipe's show read-out.** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- "the Mega Hit Blaze is getting attention, but interest is not yet the same as purchase confidence. The market is currently driven by hype and performance perception."
- JVL's **two-month grace-period financing "was not compelling enough. Customers compared it immediately with longer 0% interest and longer grace periods from competitors."**
- He **logged 21 customer conversations representing roughly 19 unique company account names**; the show is attended "mostly by the same core group of Georgia masters each year, even though the expo invites a broader audience," and maybe 50% of the ~80 operators on his list did not attend. Only 19–21 of those who did come actually approached the booth.
- **United Gaming was absent** — "There was nobody from United Gaming, which was surprising to me."
- **Aristocrat is the hot brand**, and "Some masters are hearing direct requests from locations and players for those specific brands, which makes prize [price] alone less important than expected."
- **Competitor intel: AGS is coming to Georgia next year.** Felipe first said "Scientific Gaming"; Juan corrected him — "scientific gaming is light and wonder… There is no scientific game machine anymore" — and Felipe amended it: "No, AGS, I'm sorry."

**The ILERS/ILAS report.** JVL was missing from the report circulating at the show and customers noticed — "Several people asked me why JVL was not shown in the report. Our customers even raised the issue directly with Val." Juan had already contacted ILERS: the standing arrangement is "only show our footprint, not our performance, which they agreed to do… Since if we're going to get judged on performance, we don't have enough of the new games out there to convince anybody otherwise." Felipe agreed: "for the time being, yes." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]

**Orders on the table.** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- **ARC** wants to buy the **5 machines currently on trial** and place an order for **15 more**, delivering from the **first week of September** with the cell modem installed and **V3 software**; Felipe is coordinating with [[wiki/people/cris-nacarino.md|Cris]].
- **Bulldog Gaming** — "one of the largest companies in Georgia by… machines" — had **5 machines that froze in the field**. **Brian Deaton** wants them back in mid-October (second week) so a **90-day trial** lets him decide in January and place a real order. Approved (see the decision page).

**The September/October/November forecast.** Val made Felipe insert quantity columns behind the temperature rating. The September tally came to **31 machines** — VGL "might make it 5," One GA Coin "at least three," Dixie "at least two," ARC ten more (Felipe: "he said if we're going to do it… the first week of September, we'll take 10"), Time & Amusement "maybe 4." Val confirmed those 31 need no trial. October and November were blank. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Val's market arithmetic:** "There's approximately 4 to 5000 machines sold on a yearly basis… I find it difficult to believe that out of 4 to 5000 machines, we cannot sell 4 to 500 machines per year… let's say 40 machines per month… a special at the price of $13,000." He added: "I doubt that in September you'll sell 31 machines, but let's say you do. But in October and November, I don't understand how you're going to sell machines." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Felipe's counter — the market does not plan.** "a lot of sales happen in a short period of time because people have the urgency to play machines. What I've noticed in this market is that there is not a lot of planning, but a lot of improvise… I'm getting approval for X, Y, or three locations that I didn't know I was going to get… There's movement all the time." Val: "I cannot take it to the bank." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Juan's proposal, agreed: a "show special" email blast.** "there should be a model that is so good, it's called the show special, that they can't turn it down, that you don't have to call these people. If the deal is so good, they're going to call you" — one flat price, one strong package with terms, plus a customer testimonial. Val supplied the approach for the quote: "can I use this quote from you? I'm preparing a mail blast. I want to brag about my performance," with an ARC-style quote ("I've tried 5 machines, I'm extremely happy with JVL performance… and we are buying more games"). Felipe agreed to draft it next week off the already-approved offer letter, whose stackable levers are **discount + trade-in-on-top + pay-in-full + data-sharing**. Felipe's own preference had been a filtered live review feed on the JVL webpage; Juan argued email beats forcing customers to a website. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- **What Val says he already gave Felipe:** "I gave Felipe a range of discounts. He can do trade-ins… We discussed terms for every customer. He understands the range that he can use… The only thing I didn't spell out is exactly what to do for this particular trade show. And only because I treat him as a senior individual who's capable of making decisions on the spot." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]

**Field-performance state.** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]
- **14 machines are out on trial** (Felipe), across roughly **10 masters** holding the V43 V2 (Juan).
- The **tilt-1 fix** has reached "maybe 757" — i.e. five to seven — of the ten trial masters; Felipe supplied the software and settings to all of them but "I cannot push them to do it," and show week made it worse.
- **The first test machine's data starts July 9** (Juan: "July 9th was the first test machine data that I have"), so roughly 30 days of testing data existed at the meeting. Val: "the testing data is solid."
- **The bug that was fixed** was, in Val's account, "a Linux processor Linux incompatibility issue. It was just literally impossible to even guess. We focused on the games not making money, creating new content, but at the end, it was really that the fact that machine was switching off."
- Val also noted that to get the pre-show trial ready he **cut the number of games and paid Eclipse extra** to get the approval.

**The tense close.** Val told Felipe he seemed "completely demoralized… you're so down… you have no energy… you're totally discouraged, you're broken," while "I came out from this show with excitement and with hope and I came out bullish" after walking Light & Wonder, Novomatic, IGT and Bulldog and concluding "we're in very, very good shape." Felipe: "you're right, I'm tired. I'm tired of change of directions"; Val: "maybe my mistake is including you into my exciting brainstorm sessions. But if I don't include you into my brainstorm sessions, you would be a paper pusher. I am trying to make you into a thinker." Val then left the meeting early — "I'm lost. I'm absolutely lost… My comments are not useful" — leaving Felipe and Juan to continue. **§15.8:** clear — no compensation discussed; this is performance/morale content, recorded because it bears on the sales plan. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Georgia_Machine_Sales_Strategy_Post_GOAM-August-21-2026.md]]

## Aug 26 2026 — the V3 release picks up an ~88% RTP option; the pricing theory; the test bench lands in Georgia

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-26-alignment-planning-nova-september-releases.md|Aug-26 7:40 AM ET alignment call]] (Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] calling from the Georgia office after the trade show, [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) Georgia came up four ways. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]

- **The V3 Georgia COAM release gains a lower RTP option before it ships.** [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina]] put the current package at "90, 92, 94 процента плюс 2 процента, либо 2 с половиной процента у прогрессива" and said that to set a lower percentage the games may need an **88%** option that does not exist for some of them; [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]] must add it "на вот эти 15 игр, где есть, где нету" and only then release. Val agreed and folded it into the release, expecting it to be quick for Efim — "это не часы, но это не месяца." Both September 1 products are "уже достаточно… готовые." The ruling is [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-26-add-88-percent-rtp-option-to-georgia-coam-games.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]
- **Why the lower RTP: Val's peak-hour earnings theory, parked.** From a location visit he argued JVL's high RTP (~92–93% including progressive) plus a 25¢ minimum bet caps netwin during the few peak hours a day when all machines are occupied — consistent with operators' long-standing message that JVL leads in the Georgia periphery but not in Atlanta. His proposed lever was location-based settings (lower RTP toward 90–91% and a higher minimum in Atlanta); Pavel's was hiding the lowest bet button at peak hours. **Both parked for the game-design team, no decision.** Full account on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Aug-26). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The recycler was the show's talking point, and the Georgia Lottery's chief counsel reacted strongly.** Val: the transaction recycler "был хиллаплышн. Которые просто шокировал всех," to the point that the lottery's chief lawyer "заявил, что они взволнованы этим решением." Pavel relayed [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei]]'s account: it "фурор сделал," JVL has no ready answer, "мы сейчас мышь не можем эти киоски продавать" — it cannot sell those kiosks yet — "но мы пообещали, что мы это обязательно скоро выпустим и всё будет легально." Val was content with the outcome ("Я доволен результатом… Абсолютно понятно, чётко понятно, как довести это до конца") while acknowledging the regulator's objection: they do not want the state seen as the gambler paying out money directly. The counsel is not named on this call; the InComm call the same afternoon names [[wiki/people/joe-kim.md|Joe Kim]], the Georgia Lottery general counsel who regulates COAM, as the person whose expo reaction is driving the market's posture — **the identification is an inference from role and timing, not stated in either source.** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_InComm_Skill_Pay_ATM_Guidance-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The Light & Wonder SAS test bench is now in the Georgia office**, shipped there when the South Dakota office closed. [[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth]] sets it up with Val supervising on site; [[wiki/people/anibal.md|Anibal]] (moved from Peru) is there now and [[wiki/people/sebastian.md|Sebastian]] arrives from Miami in about a week, both available for the technical testing. See [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-26-self-test-on-lw-sas-bench-in-georgia-plus-minsk-emulation.md]] and [[wiki/projects/south-dakota-vlt-market.md]] (Aug-26). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]
- **A D27 Georgia COAM version on NOVA is one of two candidate first-NOVA tracks.** Not new games but "переработка популярных игр," moving off the peach progressive to the new **Match 3** progressive (Irina supplied the name), on **D27** horizontal rather than vertical — which would require designers to support the same game on both cabinet orientations.
